We are mentioning this result here because we will discuss the analogous
representation for
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
in the following section.

4.2
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

We now study the mixed correlator with two single and two double particle insertions.
The results up to order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
were given in
[
6
]
and here we extend that analysis by
giving more details about the properties of the full correlator and the dynamical function. In the order

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

\displaystyle=16a^{2}(a+2)g_{12}^{4}g_{34}^{2}\;,

(4.26)

\displaystyle{\cal G}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

\displaystyle=1+2U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{2U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}+\frac{8}{a}\Bigg[U\sigma+\frac{U\tau}{V}\Bigg]+\frac{24}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}+\frac{4}{a}\Bigg[U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg]\;,

(4.27)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

\displaystyle={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}+\frac{2a}{a+2}{\cal H}_{2222}\;,

(4.28)

where the
16a^{2}(a+2)=8a(a+2)\times 2a
comes from the two-point functions
\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle\times\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
.

Note that
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
has an all order
\frac{1}{a}

expansion inherited from that of
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
,
{\cal H}_{2222}
,
and
\frac{2a}{a+2}
.
The first two orders of the
\frac{1}{a}
expansion are

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}\ }=\,+2{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}\;,

(4.29)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-4{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}+2{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}\,.

(4.30)

The new term at order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
is
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, and is given by
the gravity result through (
4.10
), namely

{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}=\frac{U^{2}}{V^{2}}\Psi_{B}^{(2)}\,.

(4.31)

It reads

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}=\

\displaystyle\frac{U^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg[R^{(1)}_{11}\mathcal{P}_{2}+R^{(2)}_{10}\left[z\partial_{z}-\bar{z}\partial_{\bar{z}}\right]\mathcal{P}_{2}+R^{(3)}_{11}\mathcal{P}_{1}\log V+R^{(4)}_{10}\log^{2}V+R^{(5)}_{10}\log V\log U

\displaystyle\rule{22.76228pt}{0.0pt}+R^{(6)}_{11}\mathcal{P}_{1}+R^{(7)}_{10}\log V+R^{(8)}_{10}\log U+R^{(9)}_{8}\Bigg]\,,

(4.32)

where
{\cal P}_{L}(z,\bar{z})
are the ladder integrals, see appendix
F
,
and
R^{(a)}_{k}=R^{(a)}_{k}(z,\bar{z})
are rational functions of
z,\bar{z}
with denominators proportional to
(z-\bar{z})^{k}
given in the ancillary file included with the
arXiv
submission.

A formula analogous to (
4.30
) holds in Mellin space.
As shown in
[
6
]
, the Mellin amplitude corresponding to (
4.32
) is,

{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}(s,t)=\Big[{K}(u,t)A(u,t)+\big(\psi^{(0)}(-u)-\psi^{(0)}(-t)\big)B(u,t)+C(u,t)\Big]\;,

(4.33)

where

\displaystyle A(u,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{32}{(1+s)(2+s)(3+s)}\left[-16+3ut-3s+\frac{18ut}{4+s}-\frac{18u^{2}t^{2}}{(4+s)(5+s)}\right]\;,

(4.34)

\displaystyle B(u,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{32(u-t)}{(1+s)(2+s)}\left[-\frac{1}{(1+u)(1+t)}-\frac{18ut-18(5+s)}{(4+s)(5+s)}\right]\;,

(4.35)

\displaystyle C(u,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{32}{(1+s)}\left[\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\frac{1}{2}}{(1+u)(1+t)}+\frac{6-18ut+12(5+s)}{(4+s)(5+s)}\right]\;.

(4.36)

The total amplitude
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
is the sum of
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}
and
the disconnected contributions proportional to
{\cal H}_{2222}
that can be read from the previous section, see (
4.15
).
We note that the same basis (
4.22
) that shows up for the
{\cal M}^{1l}_{2222}
is showing up also here for
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}
.

The
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
correlator satisfies invariance under (residual) crossing symmetry,
\vec{x}_{1}\leftrightarrow\vec{x}_{2}
(or
\vec{x}_{3}\leftrightarrow\vec{x}_{4}
). This means that

{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}(U,V)=\frac{1}{V^{2}}{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\left(\frac{U}{V},\frac{1}{V}\right)\;,

(4.37)

which in Mellin space corresponds to

{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}(s,t)={\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}(s,u)\,.

(4.38)

This invariance is obvious for
{\cal H}_{2222}
, however it is less obvious for
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
.
From the explicit expression of

{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}
we see that the amplitude is symmetric under (
4.38
) but out of the two possible combinations,
K(u,t)
and
K(s,t)+K(s,u)
, we only find
the first one, and similarly, we only find
\psi^{(0)}(-t)-\psi^{(0)}(-u)
and no
\psi^{(0)}(-s)
contribution.

The presence of
K(u,t)
only, implies that
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}

contributes to triple poles in
t
but does not contribute to triple poles in
s
.
In particular, the tower of the triples poles in
t
originates from the simple poles
of
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}
and the double poles of the Gamma functions in (
4.11
).
Thus we see that
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
has a
\log^{2}(V)
discontinuity,
but does not have a
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity, consequently no
\log^{2}(U)\log^{2}(V)
discontinuity.
This can be checked explicitly from the position space result by properly expanding
the transcendental functions in the small
U
, small
V
regime. The subleading
\log(U)
discontinuity instead is non trivial.

In section
5.3
we shall give a CFT explanation about the absence of
the
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity in
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, using considerations about the
{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}
OPE.
Since we will also study the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}
OPE, it is
worth mentioning that these two OPEs are different and therefore we expect to find different physics in the
two orientation of the correlators. To better understand the latter, we briefly describe
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
.

The orientation
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle
is obtained from

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

upon sending
\vec{x}_{1}\leftrightarrow\vec{x}_{4}
, ie.
U\rightarrow\frac{1}{U}
,
V\rightarrow\frac{V}{U}
.
The correlator in this case is given by

{\cal P}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}=16a^{2}(a+2)g_{12}^{2}g_{24}^{2}g_{34}^{2}\,

(4.39)

with

{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}={\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\left(\frac{1}{U},\frac{V}{U}\right)\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{\cal M}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}(s,t)={\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}(u,t)\,.

(4.40)

The Mellin amplitude
{\cal M}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}^{c}
is thus

{\cal M}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}^{c}(s,t)=\Big[{K}(s,t)A(s,t)+\big(\psi^{(0)}(-s)-\psi^{(0)}(-t)\big)B(s,t)+C(s,t)\Big]\,.

(4.41)

From (
4.34
)-(
4.36
) we find trivially that

\displaystyle A(s,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{32}{(1+u)(2+u)(3+u)}\left[-16+3st-3u+\frac{18st}{4+u}-\frac{18s^{2}t^{2}}{(4+u)(5+u)}\right]\;,

(4.42)

\displaystyle B(s,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{32(s-t)}{(1+u)(2+u)}\left[-\frac{1}{(1+s)(1+t)}-\frac{18st-18(5+u)}{(4+u)(5+u)}\right]\;,

(4.43)

\displaystyle C(s,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{32}{(1+u)}\left[\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\frac{1}{2}}{(1+s)(1+t)}+\frac{6-18st+12(5+u)}{(4+u)(5+u)}\right]\;.

(4.44)

The presence of
{K}(s,t)
now shows that
{\cal H}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
has a
\log^{2}(U)

discontinuities and in particular a
\log^{2}(U)\log^{2}(V)
contribution. In the CFT analysis of the
correlator we have to consider together both
{\cal H}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
and the disconnected
contribution from
{\cal H}_{2222}
, thus we have to add up their
\log^{2}(U)\log^{2}(V)

contributions. Then, one thing that we can look at is the fixed
s
large
t
limit of
these amplitudes, since this will have to do with the large spin limit in the block expansion.
From (
4.42
) and (
4.15
) we find

\lim_{t\gg 1}{A}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}^{c}(s,t)\sim\frac{(1+s)}{t^{2}}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt\lim_{t\gg 1}{A}^{1l}_{2222}(s,t)\sim\frac{(12+25s+15s^{2})}{t},

(4.45)

thus the behaviour of
{\cal M}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}^{c}
is subleading compared
to the one loop amplitude, therefore we expect to find different
contributions in spin when we perform the CFT analysis. We will comment on this in the
discussion section. Interestingly, each one of the rational coefficient functions of
{\cal M}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}^{c}

has the same scaling as the tree level amplitude
{\cal M}_{2222}
.

Finite difference representation.

At this point, as for
{\cal H}_{2222}
, it is interesting to ask if the one-loop contributions to
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

or
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}
can be written solely in terms of (dressed and shifted) double box functions.
In order to make a cleaner comparison with the discussion around (
4.23
) for

{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, we shall focus on the orientation
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}
, considering the possibility of rewriting the correlator as

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ij\geq 0,|i|+|j|\leq 2\end{subarray}}\frac{P_{ij}(s,t)}{(s+i)+(t+j)+1}K(s+i,t+j)

(4.46)

for some other polynomials
P_{ij}(s,t)
with
P_{ji}(s,t)=P_{ji}(t,s)
.
It is clear that results for this orientation apply to

{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
by crossing.

Recalling that
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}
contains the one-loop term (
4.23
) of
{\cal H}_{2222}
as a disconnected contribution,
we first define the correlator
{\cal H}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}
where we subtract this contributions, namely

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}

\displaystyle\equiv{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-2{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}

(4.47)

Then, from (
4.30
) we find

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ {\cal H}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}

\displaystyle={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-4{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

(4.48)

\displaystyle={\cal H}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+16U^{2}\overline{D}_{2422}(U,V)\,.

(4.49)

The Mellin amplitude
{\cal M}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
is (obviously)
{\cal M}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}={\cal M}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}-\frac{16}{(s+1)(t+1)(u+1)}
.

The superscript “
t
” in (
4.48
) stands for
tree
, since both
contributions can be understood as tree-like contributions:
{\cal M}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a}}

is a connected tree level contribution by definition, and we can think of

{\cal M}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}
as coming from the coincident limit of
tree level diagrams in a six point function, as pointed out in
[
5
]
.
The interesting result we want to present is that
{\cal M}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}
can be put in the form

{\cal M}^{t}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ij\geq 0,|i|+|j|\leq 2\end{subarray}}\frac{p_{ij}(s,t)}{(s+i)+(t+j)+1}K(s+i,t+j)

(4.50)

for some polynomials
p_{ij}(s,t)
, but
{\cal H}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}
on its own cannot! Specifically,
if one allows for an arbitrary coefficient in front of the
\overline{D}_{2422}
, compatibility
with (
4.50
) fix it to unity. This simply means that
{\cal M}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}

can be written as in (
4.50
) up to a tree level term. Let us understand this result by
repeating the discussion about the compatibility of the expression (
4.50
)
with the structure of the functions
A(s,t),B(s,t),C(s,t)
, as we did for

{\cal M}^{1l}_{2222}
around (
4.25
). In the order,

1.

Similarly to
{\cal M}^{1l}_{2222}
, we see from (
4.42
) that
the factor
(u+3)
is present in
A(s,t)
but absent from
B(s,t)
and
C(s,t)
, and that
the factor
(u+2)
is absent in
C(s,t)
.

2.

Differently from
{\cal M}^{1l}_{2222}
, the denominators of
B(s,t)
and
C(s,t)

contain also the factors
(s+1)
and
(t+1)
. These are not present in (
4.50
),
but they can be generated from shifts of
K(s,t)
, for example, see (
4.25
) and
the columns corresponding to
K(s+2,t),K(s+1,t),K(s,t+2),K(s,t+1)
and
K(s+1,t+1)
.
Even though this is an additional restriction on the
p_{ij}
, in principle, (
4.50
)
is still compatible with the amplitude.

It is remarkable that the
p_{ij}
exist such that (
4.50
) actually works.
We give them in the ancillary file included with the
arXiv
submission.
Considering the results for certain N
2
E single particle correlators obtained
in
[
86
]
, our result here
suggests that the representation (
4.50
)
might provide the natural generalization of
\overline{D}
function for a general class of one-loop amplitudes.

4.3
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

We will conclude this section by giving explicit results for

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
.
This is the first computation of a multi-particle correlator of this kind in
{\cal N}=4
SYM.
Our main purpose here will be to show how
knowledge of both
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

and (the new one)
\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

is crucial to understand new features in
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
.
The discussion here will also illustrate what is the general situation for
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
.

For the
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
we have in the order,

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}

\displaystyle=96a^{2}(a+2)(a+4)g_{12}^{6}g^{2}_{34}

(4.51)

\displaystyle{\cal G}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}

\displaystyle=1+3\Bigg[U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg]+\frac{12}{a}\Bigg[U\sigma+\frac{U\tau}{V}\Bigg]+\frac{60}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}+\frac{12}{a}\Bigg[U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg]

(4.52)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}

\displaystyle={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}+\frac{3a}{a+4}{\cal H}_{2222}+\frac{3a}{a+4}{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\,.

(4.53)

Expanding in
\frac{1}{a}
, the first two orders are determined solely by disconnected
contributions, thus they are determined by
{\cal H}_{2222}
and
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
at the relevant orders,

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}\ }=3{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

(4.54)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}=3{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-12{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}+3{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}^{2}}\,.

(4.55)

In particular, to get
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}
we also need
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}
from the previous section.

The new connected contribution
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
comes at the next order,

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-12{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+3{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}+48{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-12{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+3{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}

(4.56)

Differently from the
\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
correlator,
the gravity result for
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
now is not enough to fix the correlator
at this order because
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}
is an additional
loopy

contribution
not
determined by our gravity computation.

The position space expression for
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
is obtained from

{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}=\frac{U^{2}}{V^{2}}\Psi_{B}^{(3)}\,.

(4.57)

and reads

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}=\

\displaystyle\frac{U^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg[R^{(1)}_{15}{\cal P}_{3}(z,\bar{z})+R^{(2)}_{14}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{3}(z,\bar{z})+R^{(3)}_{15}\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(z,\bar{z})+R^{(4)}_{14}\partial_{+}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{3}(z,\bar{z})

(4.58)

\displaystyle\rule{22.76228pt}{0.0pt}+R^{(5)}_{15}{\cal P}_{2}(z,\bar{z})+R^{(6)}_{15}{\cal P}_{1}(z,\bar{z})\log^{2}V+

\displaystyle\rule{22.76228pt}{0.0pt}+R^{(7)}_{14}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(z,\bar{z})+R^{(8)}_{14}\log U\log^{2}V+R^{(9)}_{14}\log^{3}V+R^{(10)}_{15}{\cal P}_{1}(z,\bar{z})\log V

\displaystyle\rule{22.76228pt}{0.0pt}+R^{(11)}_{14}\log^{2}V+R^{(12)}_{14}\log V\log U+R^{(13)}_{15}{\cal P}_{1}(z,\bar{z})+R^{(14)}_{14}\log V+R^{(15)}_{14}\log U+R^{(16)}_{12}\Bigg]\;,

where
{\cal P}_{L}(z,\bar{z})
are the ladder integrals,
\partial_{\pm}\equiv z\partial_{z}\pm\bar{z}\partial_{\bar{z}}
,
and
R^{(a)}_{k}=R^{(a)}_{k}(z,\bar{z})

are rational functions of
z,\bar{z}
with denominators proportional to
(z-\bar{z})^{k}
given in the ancillary file included with the
arXiv
submission.

A visible feature of the position space result is the fact that only the triple box integral
{\cal P}_{3}
appears at top weight six.
This is particularly nice because at weight six more functions could have appeared. Indeed
we can contrast our result for
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
in (
4.58
) with the result
for
{\cal H}_{2222}
at two loops
[
41
]
, since both contribute to the full correlator, as can be seen from (
4.56
).
Notably,
{\cal P}_{3}
does not appear in
{\cal H}_{2222}
at two loops,
and one finds instead the weight six zig-zag integrals first introduced in
[
39
]
.
Moreover the function
{\cal H}_{2222}
at two loops is fairly more complicated.

At this point it is important to emphasise that both
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
in (
4.58
) and
{\cal H}_{2222}

at two loops enter as quantum corrections to the full dynamical function
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
,
but we find that
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
is simple! An argument to justify
this simplicity is to think about the HHLL correlator on the gravity side,
considering the idea that
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
retains features
inherited from the dual geometry, which therefore are simpler. For example, the Regge
limit computed from a geodesic in the heavy-heavy background. We investigate this in appendix
C

and show perfect match with the result coming from the correlator.
In this sense, it would be very interesting to pin down what features of the quantum
expansion of multi-particle correlators are ‘geometrical’. This might give an alternative physical
explanation to the fact that the maximal logs of
{\cal H}_{2222}
in the loop expansion do not generate a ladder series.

The Mellin amplitude corresponding to
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
is

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}^{c}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\Big[{K}_{4}(u,t)a(u,t)+\,{K}_{3}(u,t)b(u,t)\,+\tilde{K}_{2}(u,t)c(u,t)+

(4.59)

\displaystyle\rule{93.89418pt}{0.0pt}+{K}_{2}(u,t)A(u,t)+K_{1}(u,t)B(u,t)+C(u,t)\ \Big]\;,

where we have defined
25
25

25

The function
K_{2}(s,t)
is the same function as in (
4.22
) and (
4.33
), we only added a label for the transcendental weight.

\displaystyle{K}_{4}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{1}^{4}+3\Psi_{2}^{2}-2\Psi_{1}\Psi_{3}-2\pi^{2}\Psi_{2}-2\pi^{2}K_{2}-\pi^{4}

(4.60)

\displaystyle{K}_{3}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{3}_{1}(u,t)-\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{3}(u,t)+\pi^{2}\Psi_{1}

(4.61)

\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{2}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{2}-\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{K}_{2}(s,t)=-\Psi_{1}^{2}+\Psi_{2}-\pi^{2}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{K}_{1}(s,t)=\Psi_{1}

(4.62)

with
\Psi_{i}(s,t)\equiv\psi^{(i-1)}(-s)+(-1)^{i}\psi^{(i-1)}(-t)
. Note that
\Psi_{i}
carries transcendental weight
i
.
The coefficient functions
a(s,t),
…,
C(s,t)
are given in the ancillary file included with the
arXiv
submission.
For reference we quote the first two

\displaystyle a(s,t)=\frac{256}{(u+1)(u+2)(u+3)}\Bigg[

(4.63)

\displaystyle\rule{48.36958pt}{0.0pt}14-15st+\frac{9}{2}u+\frac{15st(4+5st)}{4(u+4)_{1}}-\frac{3s^{2}t^{2}(58+15st)}{4(u+4)_{2}}+\frac{45s^{2}t^{2}(1+2st)}{2(u+4)_{3}}-\frac{135s^{2}t^{2}(-2+st)^{2}}{2(u+4)_{4}}\Bigg]

and

\displaystyle b(s,t)=\frac{384(s-t)}{(u+1)(u+2)(u+3)}\Bigg[

(4.64)

\displaystyle\rule{48.36958pt}{0.0pt}46+\frac{2(10-77st)}{(u+4)_{1}}-\frac{st(116-195st)}{(u+4)_{2}}+\frac{15st(4+21st-9s^{2}t^{2}))}{(u+4)_{3}}-\frac{360st(2-st)(1-st)}{(u+4)_{4}}\Bigg]\;.

The other functions
c(s,t),A(s,t),B(s,t),C(s,t)

have a complexity similar to that of
a(s,t)
and
b(s,t)
, and in addition
c(s,t),A(s,t)
and
C(s,t)
have a contribution proportional to
\frac{1}{(s+1)(t+1)(u+1)}
.

The function
K_{2}(s,t)
in (
4.61
) is the Mellin amplitude of the double-box that we encountered already in
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
.
It is the same function as in (
4.22
) and (
4.33
), we only added a label for the transcendental weight, since now there are other functions as well.
The function
K_{4}(s,t)
is the Mellin amplitude corresponding to the triple-box, which we derive in appendix
F
, see section
F.1
.
As we mentioned around (
4.22
), in Mellin space the trascendental weight goes down by two because of the universal factor of Gamma functions.
To recover the same counting as in position space it is simple to look at the degree of the poles in
s
and
t
, and therefore at the stratification in powers of
\log(U)
and
\log(V)
,
which obviously matches the transcendental degree in position space.
The amplitude
K_{3}(s,t)
is inferred from
\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}
, and finally
{\tilde{K}}_{2}(s,t)
is inferred from
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
, see the discussion at the end of section
F.3
.

One thing to note about the coefficients functions
a(s,t),
…,
C(s,t)
, visible already in
a(s,t)
and
b(s,t)
displayed above, is the common denominator which consists of
the seven factors
(u+k)
with
k=1,\ldots 7
. In the case of
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}(s,t)
in (
4.42
)
we found the five factors
(u+k)
with
k=1,\ldots 5
, thus now we also have
(u+6)
and
(u+7)
. However, there are also structural differences compared to
the
{\cal M}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}^{c}(s,t)
in (
4.42
) and in fact one can check that a simple extension of the pattern given in (
4.24
) is now insufficient
to describe the function in Mellin space as a difference operator acting on
K_{4}(s,t)
. It would be nice to find the correct generalization.

5
Operator product expansion

In the regime where
{\cal N}=4
SYM is dual to AdS
{}_{5}\times
S
5
supergravity,
the spectrum of the theory consists of particles and multi-particle operators built out
of the supergravity fields. Stringy states are decoupled.
This simple statement puts several constraints on the spectral properties of correlators, and in particular
there are two type of consistency checks that can be considered:

(S)

Consistency with respect to the protected sector and multiplet recombination.

(L)

Consistency with respect to the anomalous dimensions of known double-particle operators.

Both types of checks are non elementary and non trivial.

Type (S) requires a small introduction. The spectrum of
{\cal N}=4
SYM is organised
into short and long multiplets. Long multiplets acquire anomalous dimension in the interacting theory, and
stringy states are those long multiplets whose anomalous dimension goes off to infinity in the supergravity limit.
As recognized in a series of seminal works
[
87
,
88
,
89
,
90
,
83
]
,
the decoupling of stringy states gives valuable information
when considered in combination with the “partial non-renormalization” theorem
[
80
]
.
To see this recall the split of the correlator
into a free and a dynamical part that follows from this theorem. See (
4.3
). Now, a free theory diagram
exchanges all the allowed operators of the theory, including the stringy states,
therefore in order to decouple the latter from the supergravity OPE
it must happen that the free theory and the dynamical function are related to each other.
This relation is particularly relevant for stringy states that in the free theory have
quantum numbers at the unitarity bound, i.e. free twist
\tau=2a+b+2
in the
su(4)
rep
[a,b,a]
, because
these states are short in the free theory. In the interacting theory all stringy states acquire anomalous
dimension, both at weak and strong coupling, and therefore in the interacting theory they recombine to form long multiplets.
However, many genuine short multi-particle operators exists at the unitary bound that instead are protected and remain in the supergravity OPE.
Therefore, when we look at the block decomposition of a free theory
diagram projected onto the unitarity bound, generically we will find a
combination of both protected multi-particle operators and stringy states.
If this is the case, in order to decouple the latter from the supergravity OPE
we have to unmix the two contributions. This is a non trivial problem because
explicit three-point couplings of the protected operators are not readily available.
Nevertheless the problem can be tackled, and it was solved
for double-particle protected operators up to order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
in
[
91
,
78
,
43
]
.
We shall see that the method developed in
[
91
,
78
,
43
]
applies to both

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle\,.

(5.1)

Instead, the discussion for
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}\rangle
with
n\geq 3
goes beyond the double particle spectrum.

We have organized the OPE analysis as follows. In sections
5.1
and
5.2
,
we discuss consistency checks of type (S), giving predictions for

{\cal H}^{c}\Big|_{\log^{0}(U)}\,.

(5.2)

In section
5.3
we discuss OPE consistency checks of type (L), giving predictions for

{\cal H}^{c}\Big|_{\log^{n}(U)}\,.

(5.3)

In section
5.4
we discuss OPE checks on
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
,
and we comment on the general case
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
with
n\geq 3
.
We shall see in particular that the gravity computation shows that three-point functions of the form
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal L}_{\tau,l}\rangle
,
where
{\cal L}_{\tau,l}
are certain long operators,
satisfy large
N
factorization formulae that are not obvious in the CFT.

5.1
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
and the twist two sector

We consider here the OPE of
{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}

and the OPE of
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
. In the limit
\vec{x}_{1}\rightarrow\vec{x}_{2}

and
\vec{x}_{3}\rightarrow\vec{x}_{4}
, their common OPE
determines the block decomposition,

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}(\vec{x}_{1},\vec{y}_{1}){\cal O}_{2}^{2}(\vec{x}_{2},\vec{y}_{2}){\cal O}_{2}(\vec{x}_{3},\vec{y}_{3}){\cal O}_{2}(\vec{x}_{4},\vec{y}_{4})\rangle={\cal P}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\sum_{\underline{R}}A_{\underline{R}}\,B_{\underline{R}}\,.

(5.4)

The
B_{\underline{R}}
are the superblocks for the operator exchanged, whose quantum numbers we are denoting collectively by

\underline{R}
. The
A_{\underline{R}}
are the four-point OPE coefficients, defined by

A_{\underline{R}}=\sum_{O,O^{\prime}\in\underline{R}}C_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]O}\ \mathbb{G}^{O,O^{\prime}}\ C_{22O^{\prime}}\;,

where
C_{p_{i}p_{j}O}
are three-point couplings and
\mathbb{G}_{O,O^{\prime}}
is the two-point function metric.
The sum over
{\underline{R}}
splits into four sectors, depending on the type of primary multiplet exchanged:

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c}\underline{R}&\frac{1}{2}\textrm{-BPS}&\frac{1}{4}\textrm{-BPS}&\textrm{semishort}&\textrm{long}\\[1.1625pt]
\hline\cr\rule{0.0pt}{17.07182pt}\textrm{operator\ label}&{\cal B}&{\cal Q}&{\cal S}&{\cal L}\\[1.1625pt]
\end{array}

(5.5)

For each multiplet exchanged there is a corresponding four-point superblock. See
appendix
D
.

The
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE is well studied, see eg.
[
87
,
88
,
89
,
73
,
91
]
,
and is contained in the
{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
OPE. The common OPE is thus the one of
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
.
It will be important to recall a couple of features of this OPE starting from the
free theory
. In the free theory we find schematically,

{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\ \sim\ 1\,\oplus\,\underbrace{{\cal O}_{2}+{\cal K}_{2}+\ldots}_{\rule{0.0pt}{6.25963pt}\tau=2}\,\oplus\,\underbrace{{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\ +\ \ldots}_{\tau\geq 4}\,.

(5.6)

where:

(1)

In the
\tau=2
sectors we find operators at the unitary bound for the
[0,0,0]
rep. The stringy states are the twist two single-
trace operators
{\cal K}_{2,l}
. The spin zero operator is the well known Konishi multiplet. From large
N
counting we expect

C_{{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2};{\cal K}_{2}}\sim\frac{1}{N}\;.

(5.7)

In the
SU(N)
theory there are
no
double particle operators at twist two.

(2)

In the
\tau\geq 4
sector we find protected double-particle operators at twist four in the
[0,2,0]
and
[1,0,1]
reps (and long operators in the
[0,0,0]
rep starting from twist four).
We point out that the protected semi-short operators at twist four are schematically of the form

{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
, with leading three-point couplings

C_{{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2};{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}}\sim 1\,.\

(5.8)

(We will need this observation in the next section.)

The free theory of the
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
correlator was given already in section
4.2
, but we recall it here below for convenience:

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

\displaystyle=16a^{2}(a+2)g_{12}^{4}g_{34}^{2}

(5.9)

\displaystyle{\cal G}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

\displaystyle=1+2U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{2U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}+\frac{8}{a}\Bigg[U\sigma+\frac{U\tau}{V}\Bigg]+\frac{24}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}+\frac{4}{a}\Bigg[U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg]\;.

(5.10)

Note that the normalized free theory
{\cal G}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
is exact in
a
.

We are interested in extracting the free theory block coefficient corresponding to the semishort operators of twist
2
in the
[0,0,0]
rep,
and from the considerations above we know that
these come exclusively from the stringy states
{\cal K}_{2,l}
.
The relevant diagrams are

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau=2\\[2.27626pt]
{\rm OPE}\end{subarray}}={\cal P}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\times\frac{8}{a}\Bigg[U\sigma+\frac{U\tau}{V}\Bigg]

(5.11)

We refer to appendix
D
for details on
how to extract the corresponding OPE coefficient.
The results is

A^{free}_{{\cal K},{2,l,[000]}}=\frac{8}{a}\times\frac{2(l+2)!^{2}}{(2l+4)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\,.

(5.12)

and it is well known in the literature.

In the
interacting theory
the stringy states
{\cal K}_{2,l}
acquire an anomalous dimensions, thus they
do not belong to the semishort sector anymore, rather they recombine to form a
long
rep.
In the block expansion this recombination is
done via a crucial relation between superblocks,

B^{{\cal S}}\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau=2\\
[000]\end{subarray}}\ =B^{{\cal L}}\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau=2\\
[000]\end{subarray}}{-}\ B^{{\cal S}}\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau=4\\
[101]\end{subarray}}\,.

(5.13)

The precise relation is given in appendix (
D.27
), as well as the
spin dependence.
26
26

26

The reason is that this formula
requires an understanding of
\underline{R}
in Young diagram
notation, which we give in app.
D
.

In practice, given the block expansion of the free theory one uses the identity
A_{{\cal K},{2,l,[000]}}B^{{\cal S}}=A_{{\cal K},{2,l,[000]}}B^{{\cal L}}+\ldots

to obtain the block expansion of the interacting theory. In the latter, the
stringy contributions appear in the long sector
{\cal L}
.
27
27

27

Not only that, recombination
also shifts the free value of certain twist
4
protected contributions.

Now that the stringy states have been recombined into the long
sector it is possible to decouple them. There are two aspects of this decoupling. First, the
dynamical function by construction contributes only to the long sector. Second,
in order for the decoupling to occur, the total long twist two contributions
A_{{\cal L},{2,l,[000]}}

must vanish, namely

A_{{\cal L},{2,l,[000]}}=A^{free}_{{\cal K},{2,l,[000]}}+{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\tau=2,l,[000]}=0\,.

(5.14)

This equation gives a non trivial prediction for
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
at twist two,
at all spins. In the space of conformal blocks this is

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a},\tau=2}\ =-\frac{8}{a}\times\frac{2(l+2)!^{2}}{(2l+4)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(5.15)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{n}},\tau=2}\ =0\rule{142.26378pt}{0.0pt}n\geq 2

(5.16)

Equivalently we can give directly the result after resummation in spin.

{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}=-8U\Bigg[\frac{(1+V)}{(-1+V)^{2}V}-\frac{2\log(V)}{(-1+V)^{3}}\Bigg]+U^{2}(\ldots)\;.

(5.17)

Both predictions are matched by our (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) as we explain below.

\bullet

Eq. (
5.15
). This follows directly from
{\cal H}_{2222}
, in particular from

{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}=-4U^{2}\overline{D}_{2422}=-4U\Bigg[\frac{(1+V)}{(-1+V)^{2}V}-\frac{2\log(V)}{(-1+V)^{3}}\Bigg]+U^{2}(\ldots)

(5.18)

and the relation (
4.29
), namely
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}=2{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}
.

\bullet

Eq. (
5.16
). Interestingly, this is a prediction only for
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
because
the one-loop function
{\cal H}_{2222}
by construction does not contribute at twist
two
[
75
,
78
]
.
28
28

28

The reason is simply
that the free theory
{\cal G}_{2222}
truncates at order
\frac{1}{a}
. See (
4.12
).
Therefore at order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
one can impose the absence of twist
two contributions simply by letting the function start at twist four. As pointed out in section
4.1
the one loop
Mellin amplitude has vanishing residue at
s=-1
, this is the simple pole corresponding to the twist two sector.

The result is non trivial and follows from a non trivial cancellation of twist two contributions

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\log^{0}(U)}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}+16U^{2}\overline{D}_{2422}\Big|_{\log^{0}(U)}=U^{2}(\ldots)\;,

i.e. the one from
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
cancel the one from

U^{2}\overline{D}_{2422}
, that as we saw above is in fact non zero.

5.2
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle
and the twist four protected operators

In this section we consider the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
OPE.
It will be convenient to apply a crossing transformation
to
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
, following (
4.40
), and write the OPE in the conventional limit
x_{1}\rightarrow x_{2}
and
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{4}
.
Therefore we will consider the block decomposition

\langle{\cal O}_{2}(\vec{x}_{1},\vec{y}_{1}){\cal O}_{2}^{2}(\vec{x}_{2},\vec{y}_{2}){\cal O}_{2}(\vec{x}_{3},\vec{y}_{3}){\cal O}^{2}_{2}(\vec{x}_{4},\vec{y}_{4})\rangle={\cal P}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}\sum_{\underline{R}}A_{\underline{R}}\,B_{\underline{R}}\,.

(5.19)

where now

A_{\underline{R}}=\sum_{O,O^{\prime}\in\underline{R}}C_{2[2^{2}]O}\ \mathbb{G}^{O,O^{\prime}}\ C_{2[2^{2}]O^{\prime}}

and again
\underline{R}={\cal B},{\cal S},{\cal L}
depending on the type of multiplet exchanged.

As for the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE, we first focus on the
free
theory.
Again we want to highlight a couple of important features
of the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
OPE, schematically summarized as follows,

{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\ \sim\ {\cal O}_{2}\,\oplus\,\underbrace{{\cal O}_{2}^{2}+{\cal K}_{4}+\ldots}_{\tau=4}\,\oplus\,\underbrace{{\cal O}_{2}^{3}+\ldots}_{\tau\geq 6}\,.

(5.20)

where

(1)

In the
\tau=4
sector we find operators at the unitarity bound for the
[0,2,0]
rep. We find both protected
double-particle operators
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
, already encountered in the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE,
and a number stringy states, denoted collectively by
{\cal K}_{4,l}
.
The precise nature of these stringy states will not be important, however, it is simple to
see that they exist. For example, we can find the double trace
{\cal K}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
and the twist four single-traces.
From large
N
counting we find that

C_{{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2};\,{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}}\sim\frac{1}{N}

(5.21)

and similarly for
{\cal K}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
,
thus we expect non trivial mixing between these operators.

(2)

In the
\tau\geq 6
sector we find both protected and long operators. The long operators belong
to the
[0,2,0]
rep and importantly, the one with leading order three-point coupling are
triple-particle operators starting
from twist six. Consequently we expect to find new CFT data that cannot be extracted from the single-particle correlators.

It follows from the first consideration above that when we extract
the block coefficient
A^{free}_{\tau=4,l,[020]}
from
the
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle
correlator
we find one coefficient for both the sum over stringy states and the protected contributions.
Instead we want to isolate the contribution due to
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
,
which is the only operator present in the supergravity OPE, and recombine
the stringy states into the long sector.
Disentangling the two contributions is non trivial
because the protected three-point couplings are not readily available.

Following
[
91
]
, we can resolve the mixing between the
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}

and the
{\cal K}_{4,l}
stringy states
by considering that the protected double-particle operators

{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
are also exchanged in the

{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE, and crucially, in the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE they are the only
twist four semishort operators exchanged both in the free and the interacting theory.
In this OPE we actually find

{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}\Big|_{[0,2,0],l=0,2,4,\ldots}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}\Big|_{[1,0,1],l=1,3,5,\ldots}

(5.22)

For the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
OPE we are interested only in the rep
[0,2,0]
.
The above considerations imply that when we assemble the Gram matrix,

G=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle&\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle\\[5.69046pt]
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle&\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle\end{array}\right)\,,

(5.23)

and we project this matrix onto the
[0,2,0]
semishort reps at twist
\tau=4
,
we must find
\det G=0
in the
interacting
theory because there is only one protected operator,
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
. Thus,
\det G=0
gives the prediction for the

{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
semishort contributions to the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}
OPE.

Let us denote
{\cal S}^{\vec{p}}_{4,[020]}\equiv A^{\vec{p}}_{{\cal S},\tau=4,l,[aba]}
the block coefficient for the various semishort contributions entering (
5.23
),
then from
\det G=0
we find

{\cal S}^{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}_{4,[020]}=\frac{\left({\cal S}^{[2^{2}]2;22}_{4,[020]}\right)^{2}}{{\cal S}^{22;22}_{4,[020]}}\;,

(5.24)

where we used
{\cal S}^{2[2^{2}];22}_{4,[020]}={\cal S}^{22;2[2^{2}]}_{4,[020]}
.
Note that this prediction is unrelated to
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle
and instead
follows from the knowledge of
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

and
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
in free theory.
The first one is given already in (
4.12
), while the latter
is a near-extremal correlator which is non-renormalized and so can be
computed in free theory, see (
D.51
). This
non-renormalization theorem is consistent with our strong coupling
results obtained from the analysis of the HHLL correlator: a non-trivial dynamical contribution to
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
would appear as a

{\mathcal{O}}(\alpha^{3})
term in (
3.43
), which is
indeed absent.

As expected from the large
N
counting of three-point functions in (
5.8
) and (
5.21
), at leading order
the denominator of (
5.24
) is
O(1)
, while the numerator
\frac{1}{a}
suppressed, thus
the overall prediction will start at order
\frac{1}{a}
.
Putting together the various
{\cal S}^{\vec{p}}_{4,[020]}
, the prediction is

\displaystyle{\cal S}^{[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}_{4,[020]}=\frac{32}{a}\frac{(1+\frac{2}{a})}{(l+3)(l+4)+\frac{4}{a}}\frac{(l+4)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\;.

(5.25)

We refer to appendix
D
for details.
We emphasise that
(
5.25
) is an exact result in
a
because
there are no other protected operators at twist four than the
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
operators.

At this point we go back to the free theory
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle

and similarly to the case of twist two in [0,0,0], (
5.13
), we proceed with multiplet recombination.
Thus, from the knowledge of the protected sector we find that the stringy contribution is,

A^{free}_{{\cal K},{4,l,[020]}}=-{\cal S}_{4,[020]}+A^{free}_{4,l,[020]}\,,

(5.26)

where
A^{free}_{4,l,[020]}
in (
5.26
) is the block coefficient of,
29
29

29

For
reader’s convenience,

\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle|_{\tau=4}={\cal P}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}\times\Big[\frac{8}{a}U\sigma+\frac{24}{a}\frac{U\tau}{V}\Big]
where
{\cal P}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]2}=16a^{2}(a+2)g_{12}^{2}g_{24}^{2}g_{34}^{2}
.

A^{free}_{4,l,[020]}=\frac{1}{a}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}+4(l+1)(l+6)\frac{(l+2)!(l+4)!}{(2l+6)!}&\ \textrm{spin\ odd}\\[14.22636pt]
+4(18+7l+l^{2})\frac{(l+2)!(l+4)!}{(2l+6)!}&\ \textrm{spin\ even}\end{array}\right.

(5.27)

Then, the constraint equation that follows from multiplet recombination and the absence of stringy states in the supergravity OPE is
that the total twist four contribution
A_{{\cal L},{4,l,[020]}}
must vanish, namely

A_{{\cal L},{4,l,[020]}}=A^{free}_{{\cal K},{4,l,[020]}}+{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}\Big|_{\tau=4,l,[020]}=0\,.

(5.28)

Explicitly,

A_{{\cal L},{4,l,[020]}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}+\frac{4}{a}(l+1)(l+6)\frac{(l+2)!(l+4)!}{(2l+6)!}&\ \textrm{spin\ odd}\\[14.22636pt]
+\frac{4(l+2)(l+5)}{a}\frac{(l+3)(l+4)-\frac{12}{a}}{(l+3)(l+4)+\frac{4}{a}}\frac{(l+2)!(l+4)!}{(2l+6)!}&\ \textrm{spin\ even}\end{array}\right.

(5.29)

As for (
5.25
), these results are exact in
a
. Further details on the computation are given in appendix
D
.

Solving the predictions for
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
in the
\frac{1}{a}
expansion, we find that at order
\frac{1}{a}
,

{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a},\tau=4}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}-4(l+1)(l+6)\frac{(l+2)!(l+4)!}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1-(-1)^{l}}{2}\\[14.22636pt]
-4(l+2)(l+5)\frac{(l+2)!(l+4)!}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\end{array}\right.\,,

(5.30)

and at order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
,

{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}},\tau=4}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}0\\[14.22636pt]
+64(l+2)(l+5)\frac{(l+2)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\end{array}\right.\,.\rule{17.07182pt}{0.0pt}

(5.31)

The results (
5.30
) and (
5.31
) after resummation are

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{22^{2}22^{2}}=

\displaystyle\,\ -\frac{8}{a}U\Bigg[\frac{(1+V)}{(1-V)^{2}V}+\frac{2\log(V)}{(1-V)^{3}}\Bigg]+U^{2}(\ldots)

(5.32)

\displaystyle+\frac{32}{a^{2}}U\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-V)^{2}V}-\frac{\log(V)+\log^{2}(V)+2{\rm Li}_{2}(1-V)}{(1-V)^{3}}\Bigg]+U^{2}(\ldots)

(5.33)

and we find a perfect match with the functions given in section
4.2
. There are several comments to highlight here:

1.

The dynamical function
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
at order
\frac{1}{a}
is
-8U^{2}\overline{D}_{2422}
,
ie. the same
\overline{D}_{2422}
as in
{\cal H}_{2222}
! But in (
5.30
) we have
to decompose it in blocks w.r.t. to the external charges
p_{43}=2
and
p_{12}=-2
and twist
\tau\geq 4
.
This decomposition is non standard compared to the literature on single-particle correlators.

2.

The spin dependence changes if we compare (
5.30
) at order
\frac{1}{a}

and (
5.31
) at order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
. This is an indication that

{\cal H}^{c}
must have new features, compared to
\overline{D}_{2422}
. In fact we see
from the resummation (
5.32
) the presence of
\log^{2}(V)
, and this cannot be obtained from a tree level function.

3.

The one-loop function
{\cal H}_{2222}
– interpreted as a contribution to
{\cal H}_{22^{2}22^{2}}
–
does not contribute to the twist four in
[0,2,0]
.
30
30

30

The fact that
{\cal H}_{2222}
has an
empty twist two sector in [0,0,0], see footnote
28
, now implies that
{\cal H}_{2222}
does not contribute to
the twist four sector in
[0,2,0]
.
The prediction (
5.31
) is directly a prediction
for
{\cal H}^{t}
, and thus for
{\cal H}^{c}
.

Worth emphasising that all the non trivial details about the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
OPE,
could not possibly work without the inclusion of the
{\cal H}^{c}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
contribution.
This provides a new consistency check of AdS/CFT in the context of multi-particle correlators.

5.3
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
and the long sector

Continuing the discussion about the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE and its implications
for
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, in this section we study what information can be used
from the long double particle sector
[
75
,
92
,
40
]
.

The relevant double particle operators will be denoted with
{\cal D}_{\tau,l}
and belong
to the
[0,0,0]
rep, studied in
[
40
]
. They exist for twist
\tau\geq 4
at any spin, and they are
degenerate in twist.
31
31

31

In
[0,0,0]
these are

\{{\cal O}_{2}\Box^{\tau-4}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2},{\cal O}_{3}\Box^{\tau-6}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{3},\ldots\}
.

These double particle operators have three-point couplings with
{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}
with the following large
N
expansion,

C_{[2^{n}][2^{n}];{\cal D}}=C^{(0)}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}];{\cal D}}+\frac{1}{a}C^{(1)}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}];{\cal D}}+\ldots

(5.34)

and their anomalous dimension, defined by

\Delta_{\cal D}=\tau+l+\frac{2}{a}\eta^{(1)}_{\cal D}+\ldots

(5.35)

are known exactly from previous work in
[
75
,
92
,
40
]
.

Our starting point is the observation that large
N
factorization relates

C^{(0)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}
and
C^{(0)}_{[2][2];{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}
,
through the obvious relation

C^{(0)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}=2\,C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}\,.

(5.36)

Then, from the OPE we deduce that

\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log^{n}(U)}{a^{n}}}=\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\tau,l}\left(\sum_{{\cal D}}C^{(0)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}}\ \eta^{n}_{{\cal D}}\ C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}}\right)\!B^{\cal{L}}_{\tau,l,[000]}=2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{\log^{n}(U)}{a^{n}}}\,.

(5.37)

The case
n=0
corresponds to the disconnected free theory diagrams contributions in (
5.9
).

From (
5.37
) follows that at each order in the large
N
expansion the function
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}-{\cal H}_{2222}
does not have a maximal logarithmic discontinuity. For example, at order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
we can define

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}\equiv{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-2{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}\,.

(5.38)

This
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

does not
have a
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity. A posteriori
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

will be contained in
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, and the claim that
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

does not
have a
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity is indeed what we have found
from the gravity computation. See section
4.2
.
At the next order we can define

{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}\equiv{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-2{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}\,.

(5.39)

This
{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}

does not
have a
\log^{3}(U)
discontinuity, and so on so forth.

We will now show that the CFT data of the non degenerate
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}

long operators
[
92
]
, gives a prediction for the
\log(U)
discontinuity of
{\cal H}^{t}
.
This follows from the two OPE equations

\displaystyle\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}{B}^{\cal L}_{4,l,[000]}+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)

(5.40)

\displaystyle\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{4}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}{B}^{\cal L}_{4,l,[000]}+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)

(5.41)

which can be derived from appendix
D.2
.

Let us begin by making some important comments about (
5.40
).
Firstly,
\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
and
\hat{\cal C}_{2222}
have a block expansion with various terms
and derivatives of the blocks. See (
D.33
) and (
D.34
). However
their difference at twist four simplifies dramatically because of the relation between leading three-point couplings in (
5.36
).
Secondly, on the lhs of (
5.40
) we find

\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}=\frac{4}{a}U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{4}{a}\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}+\frac{16}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}

(5.42)

because the
\overline{D}_{2422}
functions cancel each other.
Therefore, from (
5.40
) we obtain the result

\frac{4}{a}U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{4}{a}\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}+\frac{16}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}=\sum_{l}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\,{B}^{\cal L}_{4,l,[000]}+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)

(5.43)

which is an equation that determines
C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]{\cal D}_{4}}
from free theory data.
The result for the combination under the sum is

\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}=\frac{4}{3}(l+2)(l+5)\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\,.

(5.44)

The explicit result for
C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}
is given in (
D.61
).

Then, consider the
\log(U)
contribution at order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}
given in (
5.41
).
Since we are looking at the
\log(U)
discontinuity, there cannot be a free theory
contribution and the equation (
5.41
) is directly an equation for the dynamical
functions
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
and
{\cal H}_{2222}
, namely

\displaystyle\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}

\displaystyle={\cal I}\times\Bigg[{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}-2{\cal H}_{2222}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}\;.

(5.45)

The combination in parenthesis is precisely
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
!
Again, the block expansion of each term contains separately many
contributions but given the relation between leading three-point couplings in (
5.36
),
there is only one contribution left at twist four.
Thus, we obtain the prediction

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}=\sum_{l}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{4}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}{H}_{4,l,[000]}+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)

(5.46)

where the reduced long block
H_{\tau,l,[aba]}
is defined in (
D.21
).

At this point, we know from
[
75
,
92
]
that

\eta_{{\cal D}_{4}}=-\frac{48}{(l+1)(l+6)}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\,,

(5.47)

therefore we can predict the twist four sector

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}=\sum_{l}\Bigg[-64\frac{(l+2)(l+5)}{(l+1)(l+6)}\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\Bigg]{H}_{4,l,[000]}+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)\;.

(5.48)

Equivalently, we give its resummation

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}=

(5.49)

\displaystyle\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}-32U^{2}\Bigg[\frac{(1+10V+V^{2})}{(1-V)^{4}V}+\frac{12(1+V)\log(V)}{(1-V)^{5}}+\frac{2(1+4V+V^{2})\log^{2}(V)}{(1-V)^{6}}+U^{3}(\ldots)\Bigg]\;.

As for the remarks on the presence of
\log^{2}(V)
in
(
5.33
), we see above the presence of
\log(U)\log^{2}(V)
which is a weight three contribution beyond those of a weight two tree-level function.
Remarkably, this is precisely the same result that we obtain when we include
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, see (
4.32
).

5.4
\langle{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
and higher multi-particle correlators

In this section we study the correlator
\langle{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
and the
consistency checks that can be performed on
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
from the known OPE data. These are
the absence of twist two stringy states and the predictions for the twist four long
operators in the
[0,0,0]
rep.
32
32

32

We cannot perform type (S) checks in

\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}\rangle
without knowledge about the twist six protected
triple-particle operators in
[0,4,0]
. These determine the
\frac{1}{a}
term and then mix with
the protected double particles at higher orders.
Both checks will require knowledge from

\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
and
\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
,
and therefore will be non trivial in various ways. Specifically we will need three-point couplings relations already obtained in
5.1

and
5.3
, and new three-point coupling relations that we will derive. These checks generalize to higher multi-particle correlators

\langle{\cal O}^{n}_{2}{\cal O}^{n}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
. However, already for
n=3
, we shall see that
the analytic structure of the higher multi-particle correlators implies relations between three-point couplings that are not obvious from
the CFT.

To study
\langle{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
we will generalize the discussion
in section
5.3
.
There we undestood that the OPE
naturally splits
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
into a disconnected contribution with
maximal logarithmic discontinuities, that is
2{\cal H}_{2222}
, and the rest. At order
\frac{1}{a^{2}}

we defined the rest to be
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
. Then, the CFT analysis revealed
new predictions for
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
. Since we knew from gravity that

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-4{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

the aforementioned predictions for
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
gave constraints on
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
that we tested successfully.

Let us now consider
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
at order
\frac{1}{a^{3}}
, and generalize the reasoning above. This function was given
in section
4.3
, see (
4.56
),
where we showed that

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-12{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+3{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}+48{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-12{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+3{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}

(5.50)

The claim is that the OPE analysis singles out the following combination

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-12{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+24{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

(5.51)

and the constraint on (
5.51
) are

\displaystyle\Bigg[{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log^{3}(U)}{a^{3}}}=0

(5.52)

\displaystyle\Bigg[{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log^{0}(U)}{a^{3}},\,{\rm twist\,2}}=0

(5.53)

\displaystyle\Bigg[{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log^{n}(U)}{a^{3}},\,{\rm twist\,4}}=0\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptn=1,2

(5.54)

Very remarkably, all these predictions are satisfied when we include our gravity result for
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
.

Let us first understand why the combination (
5.51
) is singled out by the OPE analysis.
Consider arranging the expression in (
5.50
) as

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-3{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-3{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}=\Bigg[{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-12{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+24{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}\Bigg]\;,

(5.55)

where the rhs in
[\ldots]
is precisely (
5.51
) and
on the lhs we subtracted two disconnected contributions. The first one is
{\cal H}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}
, and is obvious.
The second one,
{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}
is defined by (
5.39
).
It can be computed explicitly from (
4.26
), and it is equal to

{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}={\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}+8{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-4{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}\,.

(5.56)

The crucial point about the two disconnected subtractions is that both contributions
have a twist four sector that is predicted by the OPE analysis. It follows that (
5.51
)
is the combination that is subject to the new predictions at twist four, given those for

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-3{\cal H}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}
and those for

{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}
.

Details about the OPE predictions.

Starting from (
5.52
), the fact that
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
is defined by
subtracting
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}-3{\cal H}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a^{3}}}

demonstrates that it does not have a
\log^{3}(U)
discontinuity. In fact, from large
N
factorization
we readily find that

C^{(0)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}=3C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}

(5.57)

for all double particle operators
{\cal D}_{\tau,l}
in
[0,0,0]
(see section
5.3
and
[
75
,
92
,
40
]
).
Note that (
5.57
) is the generalization of the relation

C^{(0)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}=2\,C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{\tau,l}}

that we encountered in (
5.36
).

Now let us comment on the derivation of the constraints (
5.53
)-(
5.54
).
The simplest one is (
5.53
), namely the absence of twist two stringy states.
This constraint follows from the fact
that the free theory, see (
4.52
), truncates at order
\frac{1}{a}
, and therefore
there are no twist two stringy states at the next orders,
\frac{1}{a^{n}}
with
n\geq 2
. In particular,
we must find the result quoted in (
5.53
).
33
33

33

The case
n=2
of

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
is automatically satisfied by the same for property for
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
,
noting that (see (
4.55
))

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}=3{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}+3{\cal H}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}.

The derivation of the constraints (
5.54
) instead requires
a statement about subleading three-point couplings with the twist four double
particle operator
{\cal D}_{4,l}
,

C^{(n)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}_{4}}=3C^{(n)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]:{\cal D}_{4}}-3C^{(n)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptn=1,2\,.

(5.58)

These relations can be understood as follows.
For the case
n=1
we observe that

\displaystyle\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-3\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{48}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}+\frac{12}{a}\Bigg[U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg]=3\Bigg[\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}\Bigg]\,.

(5.59)

where we used that the difference
\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}-n\hat{\cal C}_{2222}
does not have
\frac{1}{a}

dynamical funtions, i.e. the
\overline{D}
functions cancel out, and thus free theory is the only contribution.
At this point, using (
D.33
), the result follows since there is only one
{\cal D}_{4}
exchanged operator for each spin.
For the case
n=2
, we observe that

\displaystyle\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-3\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}

\displaystyle={\cal I}\Bigg[3{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-12{\cal H}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}\Bigg]=3\Bigg[\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}\Bigg]\,.

(5.60)

where we used (
4.30
) and the fact that free theory truncates at order
\frac{1}{a}
.
Then, using (
D.34
) the result follows, again because there is only one
{\cal D}_{4}
exchanged operator for each spin.

The last step to demonstrate the constraints in (
5.54
) is to look at the OPE
predictions at twist four for the
\log^{2}(U)
and the
\log(U)
of
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}-3{\cal H}_{2222}-3{\cal H}^{1l}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
.
Using the expansions quoted in (
D.35
) and the result for the
leading three-point couplings, the block expansion simplifies to

\displaystyle\Bigg[{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log^{2}(U)}{a^{3}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!=\ \tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{l}\Bigg[(C^{(1)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}_{4}}-3C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}+3C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}})\big(\eta^{(1)}_{{\cal D}_{4}}\big)^{\!2}C^{(0)}_{22{\cal D}_{4}}\Bigg]{H}_{4,l,[000]}+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)

(5.61)

At this point, the relation
(
5.58
) for
n=1
, namely

C^{(1)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}_{4}}=3C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]:{\cal D}_{4}}-3C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}

gives the result quoted in (
5.54
).
For the
\log(U)
predictions one finds a longer expression,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\Bigg[{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{3}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!=\ \sum_{l}\Bigg[(C^{(1)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}_{4}}-3C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}+3C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}})(\eta^{(1)}_{{\cal D}_{4}}C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}+\eta^{(2)}_{{\cal D}_{4}}C^{(0)}_{22{\cal D}_{4}})

\displaystyle\rule{59.75095pt}{0.0pt}+(C^{(2)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}_{4}}-3C^{(2)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}+3C^{(2)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}})\eta^{(1)}_{{\cal D}_{4}}C^{(0)}_{22{\cal D}_{4}}\Bigg]{H}_{4,l,[000]}+\ldots+\big(\textrm{twist}\geq 6\big)

(5.62)

where
\ldots
contain omitted terms involving derivatives of the twist four blocks.
Again, by using (
5.58
) for
n=2
, we find the result quoted in (
5.54
)

Absence of
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity.

Not only the twist four sector of the
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity of
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}

vanishes, but the whole
\log^{2}(U)
discontinuity. As a result, the gravity result for
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}
,
has more information than the one extracted about the twist four sector.
This is non trivial information because beyond twist four not only double particle operators
are exchanged but also long triple particle operators. The triple particle operators
{\cal T}_{\tau,l}

exist starting from twist six. These operators are degenerate both in twist and spin. Their three-point couplings with
{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}

and their anomalous dimension have the following large
N
expansion

C_{[2^{n}][2^{n}];{\cal T}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{a}}\Big[C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}];{\cal T}}+\ldots\Big]\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt\Delta=\tau+l+\frac{2}{a}\eta^{(1)}_{\cal T}+\ldots

(5.63)

Extending the analysis done in (
5.61
) we find

\displaystyle\Bigg[{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}\Bigg]_{\frac{\log^{2}(U)}{a^{3}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!=\big(\textrm{twist}\,4\big)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau,l}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\sum_{{\cal D}}(C^{(1)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}}-3C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}}+3C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}})\big(\eta^{(1)}_{{\cal D}}\big)^{\!2}C^{(0)}_{22{\cal D}}+

\displaystyle\sum_{{\cal T}}(C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal T}}-3C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}}+3C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}})\big(\eta^{(1)}_{{\cal T}}\big)^{\!2}C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22{\cal T}}\Bigg]{H}_{\tau,l,[000]}\;.

(5.64)

Setting the above expression to zero is not obvious because the operators exchanged are degenerate.
However, considering that at large spin we would find different types of contributions for the double particles and the triple particles, because of the fact that the triple particle operators are degenerate in twist and spin,
we are led to conclude that each individual contribution must vanish,
therefore

C^{(1)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}}=3C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}}-3C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptC^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal T}}=3C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}}-3C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}}\,.

(5.65)

Note that (
5.65
) are non trivial relations for
subleading
three-point
couplings that involve long operators, and that cannot be proven from four-point functions alone.

Large
N
factorization for three-point functions.

It is interesting at this point to collect the results obtained for three-point couplings, and rephrase them
as a statement about large
N
factorization for three-point functions, introducing a notion of connected versus disconnect contributions.
By this mean the following:

\bullet

For the
{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
and the double particle operators we find that

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle=\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle_{c}+4\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle

(5.66)

where the connected component is subleading and such that

\frac{\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle_{c}}{\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle}\sim\frac{1}{a}\,.

(5.67)

\bullet

For the
{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}_{3}^{2}
and the double particle operators we find that

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle=\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle_{c}+9\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle+9\big(\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle-4\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle^{\!2}\big)\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle

(5.68)

where the connected component is subleading and such that

\frac{\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal D}_{\tau,l}\rangle_{c}}{\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}\rangle}\sim\frac{1}{a^{2}}

(5.69)

\bullet

For the
{\cal O}^{3}_{2}{\cal O}_{3}^{2}
and the triple particle operators we find that

\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal T}_{\tau,l}\rangle=\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal T}_{\tau,l}\rangle_{c}+9\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal T}_{\tau,l}\rangle+9\big(\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}\rangle-4\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle^{\!2}\big)\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal T}_{\tau,l}\rangle

(5.70)

where the connected component is subleading and such that

\frac{\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal T}_{\tau,l}\rangle_{c}}{\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}\rangle}\sim\frac{1}{a^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,.

(5.71)

Given the large
N
expansion of each term, and from the knowledge of the two-point functions
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{n}{\cal O}_{2}^{n}\rangle
,
we match the results obtained from the considerations about the the four-point functions, namely

\displaystyle C^{(0)}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}];{\cal D}}

\displaystyle=nC^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}}\rule{46.94687pt}{0.0pt}n=2,3,

(5.72)

\displaystyle C^{(1)}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal D}}

\displaystyle=3C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}}-3C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptC^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}];{\cal T}}=3C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}}-3C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}}\,.

(5.73)

Let us note that factorization can only be manifested on the external
\frac{1}{2}
-BPS operators,
and in fact the same factors are there for both (
5.68
) and (
5.70
).
This is consistent with (
5.73
).

From the analytic structure of the function
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}
, in particular from
the fact that only a particular orientation of the ladder
{\cal P}_{n}
appears,
we know that out of all allowed logarithmic discontinuities,
\frac{1}{a^{n}}\log^{1}(U),\ldots\frac{1}{a^{n}}\log^{n}(U)
,
the gravity result only gives a non vanishing single logarithmic discontinuity, thus all the higher logarithmic discontinuities must
come from disconnected contributions. For
n=2,3
, this agrees with the factorization of three-point functions given above.
But this factorization pattern necessarily has to generalise to higher values of
n
in order to be consistent with the gravity result.
We find this pattern quite remarkable, since it is not entirely obvious how to prove it in the CFT.

Finally, let us note that for given value of
n
,
we only make a statement about three-point functions with long operators whose particle number
\leq n
.
For
{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
this means long double
particles (i.e. no statement about triple particles), and for

{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}
this means long double and triple particles. Considering for example
{\cal O}_{2}^{4}{\cal O}_{2}^{4}

we would then stop at the quadrupole particle operators. In each case the OPE contains higher multi-particle operators
but for these it seems that there is no meaningful separation into a disconnected contribution (as it is the case for
the
{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}
OPE and the triple particle operators, which appear
at the same order as for the
{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}
OPE).

5.5
A first look at long triple-trace operators

The mixed correlator with two double and two single particle operators
encode average CFT data for long triple trace operators, which is new, and available from
from analysing
the block expansion of the correlator in the long sector.
Here we would like to highlight two important results that follow from that analysis.

(1) In the
[0,0,0]
rep long operators are multi-particle operators with twist greater equal than four. The double particle operators are known: there is a single twist four double particle operators, and degenerate
operators starting from twist six (as we recalled in section
5.3
)
[
92
]
. Then, starting from twist six we are interested in the triple trace operators.
Say
{\cal T}
is a triple trace operators, it is expected that its leading three-point couplings are

C_{22;{\cal T}}\sim\frac{1}{N}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptC_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}}\sim\frac{1}{N}\,.

(5.74)

From the study of
{\cal H}_{2^{2}2^{2}22}
we can indeed show that

\rule{31.2982pt}{0.0pt}C_{22;{\cal T}}\neq 0\rule{14.22636pt}{0.0pt}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptC_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}}-2C_{22;{\cal T}}\neq 0\,.

(5.75)

We prove this by contradiction: if (
5.74
) is false
then the double particle operators are the only operators exchanged in the

\log(U)
discontinuity of
{\cal H}_{2^{2}2^{2}22}
at order
{\frac{1}{N^{4}}}
. Since their CFT data is known from
[
92
]

it is possible to predict this
\log(U)
discontinuity. Note that at this order
{\cal H}^{c}_{2^{2}2^{2}22}
contributes, and
special case of the twist four predictions was discussed already in section
5.3
. However,
the higher twist predictions are not consistent with the correlator. This proves the claim in (
5.75
).
See appendix
E.1
for more details.

(2) In the
[0,2,0]
rep long operators are multi-particle operators with twist greater equal than six.
The double particle operators are known
[
40
]
, and we are interested in triple particle operators.
In the correlator
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle
, we expect

C_{2[2^{2}],{\cal T}}\sim 1

(5.76)

therefore we expect triple particle operators in the disconnected free theory and in the leading logarithmic discontinuities. It will be interesting to compare with the case of
the single particle correlator
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}\rangle
, where we know that double particle operators are exchanged at leading order, since

C_{24,{\cal D}}\sim 1\,.

(5.77)

Since we cannot resolve the degeneracy of triple particle operators directly, i.e just from the knowledge of a single correlator,
it is instructive to compare the behavior at large spin between

\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}\rangle
and
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle
. This comparison can be read from Table
2
.

fixed twist

\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}\rangle

\sum C^{2}

O(l^{4})

\sum C\eta^{2}C

O(l^{2})
