D.4
Further CFT data in the long twist four sector

In this section we give additional details about the OPE analysis of the long twist four double particle operators
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
in the
[0,0,0]
rep,
that we introduced in section
5.3
.
These operators are non degenerate and therefore we can obtain their three-point couplings with the external operators by performing the OPE analysis of the four-point functions.
Specifically, in section
5.3
we obtain the relation

\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}=\frac{4}{3}(l+2)(l+5)\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(D.57)

which can be solved for
C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}
, namely

C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}=\frac{\frac{4}{3}(l+2)(l+5)\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}}{C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}+2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}

(D.58)

The authors
[
75
]
also obtained expressions for

\displaystyle C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{(l+1)(l+6)}{3}}\frac{(l+3)!}{\sqrt{(2l+6)!}}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(D.59)

\displaystyle C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{2}{3}-8(1+2\psi^{(0)}(l+4)-2\psi^{(0)}(2l+7))}{\sqrt{\frac{(l+1)(l+6)}{3}}}\frac{(l+3)!}{\sqrt{(2l+6)!}}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(D.60)

and therefore we can find
C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}
explicitly,

\displaystyle C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}=\frac{\frac{4}{3}(l+2)(l+5)+\frac{4}{3}-16(1+2\psi^{(0)}(l+4)-2\psi^{(0)}(2l+7))}{\sqrt{\frac{(l+1)(l+6)}{3}}}\frac{(l+3)!}{\sqrt{(2l+6)!}}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(D.61)

In section
5.3
, given the knowledge of (
D.57
) we deduced a prediction
for the
\log(U)
of
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, based on the known expression for the anomalous dimension
\eta_{{\cal D}_{4}}
in
[
75
]
.

Appendix E
Comments on triple particle operators

In this section we extract novel data about triple particle operators:

1. We will start with
{\cal H}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
and the triple
particle operators in
[000]
. Focusing on twist six first, we will infer the existence of such triple traces operators
from the fact that a CFT analysis based only on double particle operators
fails to make a prediction for
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
at twist six! (Instead
it did it for twist four, see section
5.3
.) The argument will be similar
to that of
[
41
, sec. 4.7]
where the authors used the conjectured
result for
{\cal H}_{2222}
at two loops. Now we can reach the same conclusion quite
explicitly already at one-loop in
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
, from a full-fledged gravity result.
A similar argument shows the existence of higher twist triple trace operators.

2. We will give formulae for the block coefficients of the leading logs of
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
.
Here the triple particle operators in
[020]
give a leading contribution, since
C_{2[2^{2}]{\cal T}}\sim 1
,
while the double particle operators are suppressed
C_{2[2^{2}]{\cal D}}\sim\frac{1}{N}
.
But let us note that triple particle operators are degenerate in spin thus we will only be able
to extract mixed quantities. It would be interesting to consider some special cases,
like spin
0,1
, but we leave this for a future work.

E.1
Proof that
C_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}\neq 0
and
C_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}_{6}}-2C_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}\neq 0

Even though the CFT data of the double particle operators
{\cal D}_{\tau}
is know from

[
75
,
92
]
, the higher than twist four OPE data entering

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
involves long triple operators, starting from twist six.
For example,

C_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal O}_{2}^{3}}\sim\frac{1}{N}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptC_{22;{\cal O}_{2}^{3}}\sim\frac{1}{N}

(E.1)

This implies that

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}{B}^{\cal L}_{4,l,[000]}+

(E.2)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \sum_{l,{\cal D}_{6}}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}{B}^{\cal L}_{6,l,[000]}+\sum_{l,{\cal T}_{6}}\Big[C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}_{6}}-2C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}\Big]C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}{B}^{\cal L}_{6,l,[000]}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +{\rm higher\ twist}

where in the second line we have both double and triple particle operators of twist six. Then we will also find

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}-2\,

\displaystyle\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}=\sum_{l}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{4}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{4}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{4}}{B}^{\cal L}_{4,l,[000]}+

(E.3)

\displaystyle\ \sum_{l,{\cal D}_{6}}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{6}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}{B}^{\cal L}_{6,l,[000]}+\sum_{l,{\cal T}_{6}}\Big[C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}_{6}}-2C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal T}_{6}}C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}{B}^{\cal L}_{6,l,[000]}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +{\rm higher\ twist}

We will now show that the

C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}\neq 0\hskip 18.49988pt{\rm and}\hskip 18.49988ptC^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal T}_{6}}-2C^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{22;{\cal T}_{6}}\neq 0\,.

(E.4)

Reasoning by contradiction let us remove by hand the triple particle contribution
from (
E.2
)-(
E.3
). If this is consistent then
we can use the knowledge about the double particle spectrum from
[
92
]
,
to predict (
E.3
), generalising the procedure in section
5.3
.
This is now a prediction for the twist six
\log(U)
contribution of

{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
that we can check to be true or false.
The relevant block decomposition is

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{\log(U)}{a^{2}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l}\Bigg[-64\frac{(l+2)(l+5)}{(l+1)(l+6)}\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+6)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\Bigg]{H}_{4,l,[000]}+

(E.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{l}\Bigg[-224\frac{(l+4)(l+5)}{(l+1)(l+8)}\frac{(l+4)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\Bigg]{H}_{6,l,[000]}+{\rm higher\ twist}\ldots

(E.6)

where we quoted also the result (
5.48
) in the first line.
It will turn out that the prediction from double particles at twist six
does not
match
the second line, thus we infer the existence of triple particle contributions,
and in particular (
E.4
) holds.

The double particle operators
{\cal D}_{6}
, of twist six in
[000]
, are schematically

O_{1}\equiv{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}\Box{\cal O}_{2}
and
O_{2}\equiv{\cal O}_{3}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{3}
.
Their CFT data is described by a matrix of leading order three-point couplings

C_{6}^{(0)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}C^{(0)}_{22;O_{1}}&C^{(0)}_{22;O_{2}}\\[5.69046pt]
C^{(0)}_{33;O_{1}}&C^{(0)}_{33;O_{2}}\end{array}\right)\,,

(E.7)

and then, for any external operator with charge higher than six, a vector of subleading three-point couplings

C^{(1)}_{pp,6}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}C^{(1)}_{pp;O_{1}}\,\,,&C^{(1)}_{pp;O_{2}}\end{array}\right]^{t}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt2p>6\,.

(E.8)

We will now need to consider the combination of three-point functions

V_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}=C_{6}^{(0)}.\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}-2C^{(1)}_{22,6}\Big]

(E.9)

whose explicit components are

\displaystyle V^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\cal D}_{6}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]

(E.10)

\displaystyle V^{(2)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\cal D}_{6}}C^{(0)}_{33;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]\,.

(E.11)

Under the assumption that there are no triple particle contributions, we can
obtain
V
from the block decomposition at twist six of the (difference of) correlators,

\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]33}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2233}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}\,.

(E.12)

Then, from the twist six disconnected contributions to the single-particle
correlators (first) and from the tree level
{\cal H}_{2222}
,
{\cal H}_{2233}
,

{\cal H}_{3333}
(second), we can compute

A_{6}=\big(C^{(0)}\big).\big(C^{(0)}\big)^{t}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptM_{6}=\big(C^{(0)}\big).\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\eta_{1}&0\\
0&\eta_{2}\end{array}\right)\!.\big(C^{(0)}\big)^{t}\,,

(E.13)

as done already in
[
92
]
. Finally, we compute

\big(M_{6}\big).\big(A_{6}\big)^{\!-1}\!\!\!.\,\,V_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\sum\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{6}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\\[8.5359pt]
\sum\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{6}}C^{(0)}_{33;{\cal D}_{6}}\end{array}\right.

(E.14)

The lhs of the above relations gives us the prediction we were looking for. This is matrix
generalization of what was done in section
5.3
for the twist four
long operators
{\cal O}_{2}\partial^{l}{\cal O}_{2}
. Let us collect all relevant formulae here below.

\bullet

The normalized free theory correlators that we need are

{\cal G}_{pppp}\Big|_{\rm disco}=1+U^{p}\sigma^{p}+\frac{U^{p}\tau^{p}}{V^{p}}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{\cal G}_{2233}=1+\frac{6}{a}\Bigg[U\sigma+\frac{U\tau}{V}\Bigg]+\frac{12}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}

(E.15)

\bullet

The normalized dynamical functions are

{\cal H}_{22pp}=-\frac{2pU^{p}\overline{D}_{p,p+2,2,2}}{(p-2)!}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{\cal H}_{3333}\Big|_{[000]}=-\frac{3}{2}U^{p}\Big[\overline{D}_{2,5,2,3}+\overline{D}_{2,5,3,2}+6\overline{D}_{3,5,2,2}+8\overline{D}_{3,5,3,3}\Big]

(E.16)

\bullet

A simple computation gives the matrices

\displaystyle A_{6}

\displaystyle=+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{10}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{40}(l+2)(l+7)\end{array}\right)(l+1)(l+8)\frac{(l+4)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}

\displaystyle M_{6}

\displaystyle=-6\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}4&12\\
12&44+9l+l^{2}\end{array}\right)\frac{(l+4)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}

At this point we need to compute the vector
V
. For the first component we need to
consider
{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}
as in (
5.42
), and we
will do the twist 6 computation shortly. For the second component,
V^{(2)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}
,
we need to consider the correlator
{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]33}
at order
\frac{1}{a}
.
This particular contribution is still disconnected, and therefore it is
fully determined by free theory and by
{\cal C}_{2233}
at order
\frac{1}{a}
. In fact,

{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]33}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}=2{\cal C}_{2233}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}

(E.21)

Thus
V^{(2)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}=0
. For completeness we quote the free theory computed by Wick contractions

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]33}

\displaystyle=g_{12}^{4}g_{34}^{3}{\cal N}_{[2^{2}]}{\cal N}_{3}

(E.22)

\displaystyle{\cal G}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]33}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{12}{a}\Bigg[U\sigma+\frac{U\tau}{V}\Bigg]+\frac{24}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}+\frac{24}{a(a+2)}\Bigg[U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}+\frac{4U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}+2\Bigg[\frac{U^{3}\sigma^{2}\tau}{V}+\frac{U^{3}\sigma\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}\Bigg]\Bigg]

where the two point function normalization is
{\cal N}_{[2^{2}]}{\cal N}_{3}=\frac{24(N^{2}-1)(N^{2}-4)(N^{4}-1)}{N}
.
It is then obvious from (
E.15
) that
{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]33}|_{\frac{1}{a}}=2{\cal C}_{2233}|_{\frac{1}{a}}
.
This should be contrasted with the case

\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}-2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}\Big|_{\frac{1}{a}}=\frac{4}{a}U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{4}{a}\frac{U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}}+\frac{16}{a}\frac{U^{2}\sigma\tau}{V}\,,

(E.23)

already used in (
5.42
).
38
38

38

The crucial point of this mismatch is the fact that even
though a diagram of
\langle{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}^{2}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
is decomposable into
factors of
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
and
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
, the latter
has full crossing symmetry and so the color factors, however
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle
is not full crossing.
This implies that generically
\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a}}\neq 2\,\hat{\cal C}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a}}
. In fact, upon
explicit computation the equality holds only in certain cases: for the
\gamma=2
diagrams, and for the
\gamma=4
disconnected diagrams but only
O(1)
contributions.

From (
E.23
) the computation of
V^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}

follows from a simple
\gamma=4
free theory block expansion. The final result is

V_{[2^{2}][2^{2}],6}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle\frac{4+9l+l^{2}}{5}\frac{(l+4)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\,\,,&0\end{array}\right]\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\,.

(E.24)

Therefore we get the prediction

\sum\Big[C^{(1)}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}];{\cal D}_{6}}-2C^{(1)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}\Big]\eta_{{\cal D}_{6}}C^{(0)}_{22;{\cal D}_{6}}=-96\frac{(l^{2}+9l+4)}{(l+1)(l+8)}\frac{(l+4)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(E.25)

which
does not match
the result in (
E.5
). This proves
the existence of long triple traces contributions at twist six in
[0,0,0]
.

Finally, note that the our same argument here can be generalised to higher twists showing the existence of triple traces contributions at higher twist in
[0,0,0]
.

E.2
Data for the long sector in
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle

The block decomposition of the
\frac{1}{a^{n}}\log^{n}(U)
discontinuities
of
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}^{2}\rangle

contain new CFT data.
In particular, CFT data for triple particle operators in the
[0,2,0]
rep.
Let us recall that given a function
{\cal H}
, its block decomposition will be given by

{\cal H}=\sum_{\tau,l,[aba]}A_{\tau,l,[aba]}{H}_{\tau,l,[aba]}

(E.26)

where the functions
{H}_{\tau,l,[aba]}
have been given in (
D.21
). We are interested in computing
the
A_{\tau,l,[020]}
as function of twist and spin, for the
function
{\cal H}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}
. Since
[0,2,0]
is the only available rep, in the following we shall use
A_{\tau,l}\equiv A_{\tau,l,[020]}
.

To describe the block coefficients,
A_{\tau,l}
, it will be useful to introduce some definitions,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\tau,l}=\frac{(\frac{\tau}{2}+1)!^{2}(l+\frac{\tau}{2})!^{2}}{48\tau!(2l+\tau+2)!}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptT=\frac{\tau+1}{2}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptL=l+\frac{\tau+3}{2}

(E.27)

and write the block coefficients in the factorized form
A_{\tau,l}=\mathbb{F}_{\tau,l}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau,l}
39
39

39

Using blocks with
(-1)^{l}
.

[n=0]
The relevant disconnected free theory is
{\cal P}_{2[2^{2}]2[2^{2}]}(U^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{2U^{2}\tau^{2}}{V^{2}})
. We find

\mathbb{R}_{\tau,l}=(l+1)(2l+\tau+4)(2l+\tau+2)(\tau+2+l)\left((-1)^{l}\tfrac{96}{\tau(\tau+2)}-\tfrac{1}{4}+L^{2}\right)

(E.28)

Note that
\mathbb{R}_{\tau,l}
is invariant under
l\rightarrow-l-\tau-3
at fixed
\tau
,
and under
\{\tau\rightarrow-\tau-2,l\rightarrow-l-2\}
. For illustration, the twist six line is

{A}_{6,l}=\frac{2}{15}(l+1)(l+4)(l+5)(l+8)\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}(l+3)(l+6)+2\tfrac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\Big]\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}

(E.29)

[n=1]
As we pointed out in the main text, the dynamical function is
-8U^{2}\overline{D}_{2422}
where
the
\overline{D}_{2422}
is (very) familiar from the stress tensor correlator. However here we have to
perform a non standard decomposition of the
\log(U)
sector, done with respect to blocks in
which
p_{12}=-2,p_{43}=2
and the minimum twist is six. The result is new, and reads

\mathbb{R}_{\tau,l}=\mathbb{R}^{-}_{\tau,l}\frac{1-(-1)^{l}}{2}+\mathbb{R}^{+}_{\tau,l}\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}

(E.30)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{-}_{\tau,l}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{(-4+\tau)(-2+\tau)(\tau+4)(\tau+6)}{2}\left[(l+1)(l+\tau+2)(L^{2}-\tfrac{96}{\tau(\tau+2)}-\tfrac{3}{5}T^{2}+\tfrac{71}{10})\right]

(E.31)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{+}_{\tau,l}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{R}^{-}_{\tau,l}-24\tfrac{(-4+\tau)(-2+\tau)(\tau+4)(\tau+6)}{\tau(\tau+2)}(2+2l+\tau)(4+2l+\tau)

(E.32)

For illustration,
the twist six line is

{A}_{6,l}=-8\Bigg[(l+2)(l+7)(16+9l+l^{2})\tfrac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}+(l+1)(l+3)(l+6)(l+8)\tfrac{1-(-1)^{l}}{2}\Bigg]\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\

(E.33)

[n=2,
{\cal H}^{t}
]

The dynamical function here is
{\cal H}^{c}_{[2^{2}][2^{2}]22}|_{\frac{1}{a^{2}}}-4{\cal H}_{2222}|_{\frac{1}{a}}
. The first line at twist six is

{A}_{6,l}=32(-1)^{l+1}\Bigg[\frac{3(l+1)(l+3)(l+6)(l+8)}{(l+4)(l+5)}-((2l+9)^{2}-37)\tfrac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\Bigg]\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\

The block coefficients at higher twists are a bit more complicated,
but we manage to write them in the following form

\mathbb{R}_{\tau,l}=32\frac{(l+1)(l+\tau+2)}{\tau^{2}(\tau+2)^{2}}(-1)^{l+1}\Big[2(\tfrac{\tau}{2}-2)_{6}\Big[96+\tfrac{8}{15}(\tfrac{\tau}{2}-2)_{6}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}(1+\tfrac{3}{(l+1)(l+\tau+2)})\big)\Big]+\Sigma_{\tau,l}\Big]

where

(E.36)

where
I=i-T+3
and

\displaystyle B_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-5+I+T)(-3+I+T)(3+I+T)(5+I+T)(1191-71(I+T)^{2})}{92400}

(E.37)

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{-508221+100379(I+T)^{2}-2979(I+T)^{4}-59(I+T)^{6}}{831600}

(E.38)

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{32445-2979(I+T)^{2}-1330(I+T)^{4}+64(I+T)^{6}}{831600}

(E.39)

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{-639-59(I+T)^{2}+64(I+T)^{4}+4(I+T)^{6}}{831600}

(E.40)

The symmetry is
I\rightarrow-I
and
T\rightarrow-T
which correspond to
i\rightarrow-i-6
and
\tau\rightarrow-\tau-2
.

[n=2,
{\cal H}_{2222}
]

The above results add up to the contributions from

.
We will only give the twist six line

\displaystyle{A}_{6,l}=-192\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\rule{28.45274pt}{0.0pt}\Big[(82+45l+5l^{2})-\frac{16}{(l+3)}+\frac{16}{(l+6)}-4(l+2)(l+7){\rm HarmonicNumber}(l+4)\Big]\tfrac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}+

A quick comparison with
\langle{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{4}\rangle

The CFT data presented above is not available from single-particle correlator.

\begin{array}[]{c|cr}\hline\cr\rule{0.0pt}{22.76228pt}\textrm{\bf[n=0]}&&\frac{1}{15}(l+1)(l+4)^{2}(l+5)^{2}(l+8)\frac{(l+3)!^{2}}{(2l+8)!}\\[7.74997pt]

Appendix F
Mellin space, ladders and holographic correlators

In the main text we used Mellin space as the natural framework to understand features of the
multi-particle correlators in both AdS
5
and AdS
3
. Here we give additional details on their Mellin amplitudes.

Let us first recall some basics.
In both AdS
5
and AdS
3
, superconformal symmetry implies that a correlator of four
\frac{1}{2}
-BPS
operators is given by a free (or generalized free) contribution, and a dynamical function.
The latter has a well defined Mellin tranform. We shall focus on next-to-next-to extremal correlators, since these are the
multi-particle correlators that we discussed in the main text.

AdS
3
.

By following the conventions of (
4.1
) with
{\cal P}=g_{12}^{n}g_{34}
, we have

\hat{\cal C}_{[1^{n}][1^{n}]11}={free}+\frac{(x_{1}-y_{1})(x_{2}-y_{2})}{y_{1}y_{2}}{\cal H}_{[1^{n}][1^{n}]11}(U,V)

(F.1)

where

{\cal H}_{[1^{n}][1^{n}]11}=\oint\!\!\frac{dsdt}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\,\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[s+t+2]^{2}U^{1+s}V^{t}{\cal M}_{[1^{n}][1^{n}]11}(s,t)\\

(F.2)

The natural
u
variable here is
u=-s-t-2
.

AdS
5
.

By following the conventions of (
4.1
) with
{\cal P}=g_{12}^{2n}g_{34}^{2}
, we have

\hat{\cal C}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}={free}+\frac{(x_{1}-y_{1})(x_{1}-y_{2})(x_{2}-y_{1})(x_{2}-y_{2})}{(y_{1}y_{2})^{2}}{\cal H}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}(U,V)

(F.3)

where

{\cal H}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}=\oint\!\!\frac{dsdt}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\,\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[s+t+4]^{2}U^{2+s}V^{t}{\cal M}_{[2^{n}][2^{n}]22}(s,t)

(F.4)

The natural
u
variable here is
u=-s-t-4
.

F.1
Ladders in Mellin space

The series of 4pts ladder integrals
{\cal P}_{L}(z,\bar{z})
is based on the well known formula
[
121
]

{\cal P}_{L}(z,\bar{z})=\sum_{r=0}^{L}\frac{(-1)^{r}}{r!}B\!\Big[\,^{2L-r}_{\ \ L}\Big]\log^{r}(z\bar{z})\Big({\rm Li}_{2L-r}(z)-{\rm Li}_{2L-r}(\bar{z})\Big)

(F.5)

where
B[\,^{a}_{b}]=\frac{a!}{b!(a-b)!}
is Newton’s binomial.
We quote the first few integrals for reference,

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{0}(z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=-\frac{z}{z-1}+\frac{\bar{z}}{\bar{z}-1}

(F.6)

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{1}(z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\ \log(z,\bar{z})\big(\log(1-z)-\log(1-\bar{z}))+2\big({\rm Li}_{2}(z)-{\rm Li}_{2}(\bar{z})\big)

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{2}(z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log^{2}(z,\bar{z})\big({\rm Li}_{2}(z)-{\rm Li}_{2}(\bar{z})\big)-3\log(z,\bar{z})\big({\rm Li}_{3}(z)-{\rm Li}_{3}(\bar{z})\big)+6\big({\rm Li}_{4}(z)-{\rm Li}_{4}(\bar{z})\big)

The
{\cal P}_{L}
have a symmetry,

{\cal P}_{L}(\tfrac{1}{z},\tfrac{1}{z})=-{\cal P}_{L}(z,\bar{z})\,,

(F.7)

and therefore
under crossing transformations there are three (instead of six) independent orientations, namely

{\cal P}_{L}(z,\bar{z})\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{\cal P}_{L}\!\left(\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{\bar{z}}{\bar{z}-1}\right)\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt{\cal P}_{L}(1-z,1-\bar{z})\,.

(F.8)

The case of
{\cal P}_{L=1}
is special since it is singlet under crossing.

In this section we give explicit formulae for the Mellin transform of

\Phi^{(L)}(z,\bar{z})=-\frac{1}{z-\bar{z}}\,{\cal P}_{L}\!\left(\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{z}{\bar{z}-1}\right)

(F.9)

The reason why we prefer this orientation will become soon clear. For the moment let us note that the
differential-recurrence relations for the
{\cal P}_{L}
gives corresponding relations for the
\Phi^{L}(z,\bar{z})
:

\frac{UV}{z-\bar{z}}\partial_{z}\partial_{\bar{z}}\Big[(z-\bar{z})\Phi^{L}(z,\bar{z})\Big]=-\Phi^{(L-1)}(z,\bar{z})\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988pt\Phi^{(0)}=1

(F.10)

where we introduced

U=(1-z)(1-\bar{z})\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptV=z\bar{z}\,,

(F.11)

and let us also note that the symmetry of
{\cal P}_{L}
translates into

\Phi^{(L)}(1-z,1-\bar{z})=\Phi^{(L)}(z,\bar{z}),

(F.12)

which is the symmetry
U\leftrightarrow V
, also manifest in the differential equation.

We focus on the orientation
\Phi^{(L)}
because it manifests the maximal logs in
U
and
V
,
and this is a property that will turn out to be useful when we consider the Mellin transform. In particular,

\Phi^{(L)}(z,\bar{z})\Big|_{\log^{L}(U)\log^{L}(V)}=\frac{1}{(L!)^{2}}\frac{1}{z-\bar{z}}

(F.13)

where a
{1}/{L!}
is explicit in (
F.5
) with
r=L
, while the other
{1}/{L!}
comes from
expanding
{\rm Li}_{L}(z)-{\rm Li}_{L}(\bar{z})
.

More precisely,
\Phi^{(L)}(z,\bar{z})
has an expansion in small
U
and small
V
that follows from
expanding the polylogs (
F.5
) in the small
\bar{z}
and small
\zeta
variables, where

z=1-\zeta\,,

(F.14)

using the identities,

\displaystyle\zeta=

\displaystyle\ U+\sum_{n,m\geq 1}\frac{U^{n}}{n!}\frac{V^{m}}{m!}\frac{(m+n-1)!(m+n-2)!}{(n-1)!(m-1)!}\,,

(F.15)

\displaystyle\bar{z}=

\displaystyle\ V+\sum_{n,m\geq 1}\frac{U^{n}}{n!}\frac{V^{m}}{m!}\frac{(m+n-1)!(m+n-2)!}{(n-1)!(m-1)!}\,.

(F.16)

For example, in (
F.13
) it is simple to find that,

\Phi^{(L)}(U,V)\Big|_{\log^{L}(U)\log^{L}(V)}=\frac{1}{(L!)^{2}}\,\frac{1}{1-\zeta-\bar{z}}=\frac{1}{(L!)^{2}}\sum_{m,n,\geq 0}\frac{U^{n}V^{m}}{n!m!}\times\frac{(m+n)!^{2}}{n!m!}

(F.17)

The more general formula for
(1-\zeta-\bar{z})^{-p}
with
p\in\mathbb{N}
is given in (
F.28
).

Turning to Mellin space, we consider

\Phi^{(L)}=\oint\frac{U^{s}V^{t}}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\,\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[-u]^{2}\,{\cal K}^{(L)}(s,t)\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptu=-s-t-1\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptL\geq 1

The
U\leftrightarrow V
symmetry implies that
K(s,t)=K(t,s)
.
The differential operator (
F.10
) turns into the following difference operator

\Delta_{st}[{\cal K}^{(L)}]:=-st{\cal K}^{(L)}[s,t]+\frac{s^{2}t}{s+t}{\cal K}^{(L)}[s-1,t]+\frac{t^{2}s}{s+t}{\cal K}^{(L)}[s,t-1]

(F.18)

and the differential-recurrence equation becomes

\Delta_{st}[{\cal K}^{(L)}]=-{\cal K}^{(L-1)}(s,t)\,.

(F.19)

In Mellin space we have to start the recurrence from the
L=1
integral, since
\Phi^{(0)}=1
has vanishing Mellin transform.
The reason is that
\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[-u]^{2}
can be assigned
transcendentals weight
2
, thus
{\cal K}^{(1)}
is a constant and becomes the starting point of the recursion.
In particular

{\cal K}^{(1)}=1;

(F.20)

and one can check that
\Delta_{st}[1]=0
.

For
L>1
, we expect
{\cal K}
to have transcendental weight
2L-2
,
in order to make manifest the uniform transcendentality of the ladders, that equals
2L
.
Moreover, the condition (
F.17
) translates into a condition on the maximal poles of
{\cal K}^{(L)}
, which can only be of the form,

{\cal K}^{(L)}(s,t)=\frac{1}{(s-n)^{L-1}(t-m)^{L-1}}\ +\ {\rm lower\ order\ poles}\hskip 18.49988ptn,m\in\mathbb{N}\;.

(F.21)

Note that the
\frac{1}{(L!)^{2}}
in (
F.17
) comes from integration by parts.

Solutions

The first few integrals are

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{(2)}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(2!)^{2}}\bigg[-\Psi_{1}^{2}+\Psi_{2}-\pi^{2}\bigg]

(F.22)

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{(3)}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{(3!)^{2}}\bigg[+\Psi_{1}^{4}+3\Psi_{2}^{2}-2\Psi_{1}\Psi_{3}-2\pi^{2}\Psi_{2}-4\pi^{2}{\cal K}^{(2)}-\pi^{4}\bigg]

(F.23)

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{(4)}(s,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{(4!)^{2}}\bigg[-\Psi_{1}^{6}-3\Psi_{1}^{4}\Psi_{2}-9\Psi_{1}^{2}\Psi_{2}^{2}+9\Psi_{2}^{3}+4\Psi_{1}^{3}\Psi_{3}-12\Psi_{1}\Psi_{2}\Psi_{3}-4\Psi_{3}^{2}+3\Psi_{1}^{2}\Psi_{4}+3\Psi_{2}\Psi_{4}+

\displaystyle\rule{48.36958pt}{0.0pt}+\pi^{2}(6\Psi_{1}^{2}\Psi_{2}-12\Psi_{2}^{2}+6\Psi_{1}\Psi_{3}-\Psi_{4})-9\times 4\times\pi^{2}{\cal K}^{(3)}-6\pi^{4}{\cal K}^{(2)}-\pi^{6}\bigg]

(F.24)

where we defined

\Psi_{n}(s,t)={\tt PolyGamma}[n-1,-s]+(-1)^{n}{\tt PolyGamma}[n-1,-t]\,.

(F.25)

In the main text we used
K_{2}(s,t)=2{\cal K}^{(2)}
in (
4.18
), then we used
K_{4}(s,t)=12{\cal K}^{(3)}
in (
4.60
).

F.2
Maximal log trick for sugra amplitudes

Our AdS correlators
{\cal C}(z,\bar{z})
have the form of a sum over ladder integrals, and derivatives of ladder
integrals, each one multiplied by a certain rational function.
Focus on the orientation of the correlator that manifest the max logs
\log^{L}(U)\log^{L}(V)
and
take the following projection

{\cal C}(z,\bar{z})\Big|_{\frac{\log^{L}(U)\log^{L}(V)}{(L!)^{2}}}=\frac{P(U,V)}{\ \ (z-\bar{z})^{2\gamma+1}}\;,

(F.26)

where the polynomial
P(U,V)
will be written as

P(U,V)=\sum_{a,b}c_{ab}U^{a}V^{b}\hskip 18.49988pt;\hskip 18.49988ptc_{ab}\in\mathbb{R}

(F.27)

We now show that quite straightforwardly we can reconstruct
the top weight contribution of the Mellin amplitude
{\cal M}
, i.e. the rational function
in
s,t
that will multiply
{\cal K}^{(L)}
. To this end we only need to
consider the following identity

\frac{{U}^{a}\,{V}^{b}}{(z-\bar{z})^{2\gamma+1}}=\frac{\gamma!}{(2\gamma)!}\oint(-{U})^{s}(-{V})^{t}\ \frac{\Gamma[-s]\Gamma[-t]}{\Gamma[s+1]\Gamma[t+1]}\Gamma[s+t+X]^{2}\times m_{ab,\gamma}

(F.28)

where

m_{ab,\gamma}=\left[(-)^{a+b}\frac{(-s)_{a}(-t)_{b}}{(s+1)_{\gamma-a}(t+1)_{\gamma-b}}(s+t+X)_{2\gamma+1-a-b-X}(s+t+X)_{\gamma+1-a-b-X}\right]\hskip 18.49988pt

(F.29)

with
X
corresponding to the definition of the
u
variable in Mellin space, and apply it repeatedly to all terms of
P(U,V)
. Then,

A(s,t)=\sum_{a,b}c_{ab}m_{ab,\gamma}

(F.30)

because

\oint\frac{U^{s}V^{t}}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\,\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[-u]^{2}\,\Bigg[{\cal K}^{(L)}(s,t)A(s,t)+\ldots\Bigg]=\frac{\log^{L}(U)\log^{L}(V)}{(L!)^{2}}\frac{P(U,V)}{\ \ (z-\bar{z})^{2\gamma+1}}+\ldots

(F.31)

The non maximal log contributions to the Mellin amplitude cannot be found this way, it is nevertheless important to have this as a starting point.

F.3
Transcendental basis for
\langle{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}^{3}{\cal O}_{2}{\cal O}_{2}\rangle

The dynamical function
{\cal H}^{t}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}

has maximum transcendental weight six given by
the ladder integral
{\cal P}_{L=3}
.
In this section we describe its lower weight
completion in the basis of svhpl functions,
from weight five (antisymmetric) to weight zero.
This basis is closely related to (a subset of)
the basis that is used for
{\cal H}_{2222}
at two-loops, studied in
[
41
,
95
]
.
However, the
\log^{3}(U)
discontinuity of
the two-loop function
{\cal H}_{2222}

does not have a contribution from
{\cal P}_{L=3}
.
Instead, its top weight six is given by the zig-zag
integrals and therefore the authors of

[
41
]
tailored the lower weights basis
to the weight six zig-zag integrals. Here we
will give a version of the basis tailored to
{\cal P}_{L=3}
.
Before doing so, let us also observe that
from the structure of BMN respresentation
of our HHLL correlator, see (
3.43
)-(
3.45
),
we expect to find even a restricted basis,
simply made from ladders, derivatives of the ladders and simple logs.

The simplest base elements are those at weight 0,1,2,3.
This is because the OPE predicts a
\log^{3}(U)

discontinuity at order
\frac{1}{a^{3}}
,
as the maximal
\log
discontinuity.
Therefore, all svhpl functions up to weight

3
have to be included. We summarize them in the following table,

\begin{array}[]{c|ll}\textrm{weight}&\textrm{anti-symmetric functions}&;\ \ \,\textrm{symmetric functions}\\
\hline\cr\\
0&&;\ \ \,1\\[7.74997pt]
\hline\cr\\
1&&;\ \ \,\log(V)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log(U)\\[7.74997pt]
\hline\cr\\
2&\ {\cal P}_{1}&;\hskip 9.24994pt\log^{2}(V)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log(U)\log(V)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log^{2}(U)\\[7.74997pt]
\hline\cr\\
3&\ {\cal P}_{1}\log(V)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{1}\log(U)&;\hskip 9.24994pt\log^{3}(V)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log(U)\log(V)\log(UV)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log^{3}(U)\\[7.74997pt]
&&\ \ \ \ \,\log(U)\log(V)\log(\frac{U}{V})\\[7.74997pt]
&&\ \ \ \ \,\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(1-z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(\frac{z}{z-1})\end{array}\vskip 5.69054pt

(F.32)

For the same reason, all weight
4^{-}
svhpl antisymmetric functions have to be included

\begin{array}[]{c|lll}\textrm{weight}&\textrm{functions}\\
\hline\cr\\
4^{-}&\ {\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(V)&,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)&,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(\frac{U}{V})\\[7.74997pt]
&\ {\cal P}_{2}(1-z)&,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{2}(z)&,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{2}(\frac{z}{z-1})\end{array}

(F.33)

Starting from weight
4^{+}
, there will be restrictions. The
complete weight
4^{+}
basis goes up to
\log^{4}
terms, eg. it contains all products
\log^{m}(U)\log^{n}(V)
such that
m+n=4
.
But for our purpose we need to truncate it such that at most we find
\log^{3}
.
To do so let us start by writing the complete basis of weight
4^{+}
symmetric svhpl functions, which consists of the following 12 elements,

\begin{array}[]{c|l}\textrm{weight}&\textrm{functions}\\
\hline\cr\\
4^{+}&\ {\cal P}_{1}^{2}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\cancel{\log(U)\log(V)(\log^{2}(U)+\log^{2}(V))}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\cancel{\log^{4}(V)}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\cancel{\log^{4}(U)}\\[7.74997pt]
&\rule{45.52458pt}{0.0pt}\log(U)\log^{2}(V)\log(\frac{U}{V})\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log^{2}(U)\log(V)\log(\frac{U}{V})\\[7.74997pt]
&\ \zeta_{3}\log(V)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\zeta_{3}\log(U)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log(UV)\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(z)\\[7.74997pt]
&\ \partial_{-}\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(1-z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\partial_{-}\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\partial_{-}\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(\frac{z}{z-1})\end{array}

(F.34)

A way to truncate the basis to
\log^{3}
elements is to take a
linear combination
v=\sum_{i}a_{i}b_{i}
, where
b_{i}
are all the basis elements, impose that there are no
\log^{4}
terms, and repeat the process
for all the other crossing images of
v
. By doing so the
elements
are excluded.

Proceeding in a similarly way at weight
5^{-}
, we list the complete basis of weight
5^{-}
antisymmetric svhpl functions first, which consists of

\begin{array}[]{c|ll}\textrm{weight}&\textrm{functions}\\
\hline\cr\\
5^{-}&\ \cancel{{\cal P}_{1}\log^{3}(V)}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\cancel{{\cal P}_{1}\log(U)\log(V)\log(UV)}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\cancel{{\cal P}_{1}\log^{3}(U)}\\[7.74997pt]
&\ {\cal P}_{1}\log(U)\log(V)\log(\frac{U}{V})\\[7.74997pt]
&\ {\cal P}_{1}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(1-z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{1}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt{\cal P}_{1}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{2}(\frac{z}{z-1})\\[7.74997pt]
&\ \log(U){\cal P}_{2}(1-z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log(V){\cal P}_{2}(z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\log(UV){\cal P}_{2}(\frac{z}{z-1})\\[7.74997pt]
&\ \partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(1-z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(z)\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(\frac{z}{z-1})\end{array}

(F.35)

and as in the case of
4^{+}
, the
terms
are excluded.

It turns out that out the position space result for the correlator
{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}^{t}
is spanned
by an even restrict set of basis elements, namely

{\cal H}_{[2^{3}][2^{3}]22}^{t}={\rm span}\Big\{{\cal P}_{3}\,,\,\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{3}\,,\,\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}\,,\,\partial_{+}\partial_{-}{\cal P}_{3}\Big\}\cup\Big\{\,4^{-},3,2,1,0\Big\}

(F.36)

where the first parenthesis
\{\ldots\}
contain weight
6,5^{\pm},4^{+}
, but only in the form of
{\cal P}_{3}
and its derivatives.

In order to conclude our discussion here we need to understand how to map (
F.36
) to Mellin space.
We derived already in (
F.22
) the amplitudes corresponding to
{\cal P}_{3}
and
{\cal P}_{2}
,
thus we only need to understand
\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}
and
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
in the weight
4^{-}

basis, the rest of the basis will follow from the one-loop basis. For the function

{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
we can make a preliminary comment. Recall that that at
one-loop the term
{\cal P}_{1}\log(U)
turned out to imply the presence of the
amplitude
\Psi_{1}(s,t)
. The justification in this case is simply that
{\cal P}_{1}
has a constant
Mellin amplitude, unity, thus in order to capture
\log^{2}U\log V
one has to add weight
one polygammas, namely
\Psi_{1}(s,t)
. Now,
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
has
\log^{3}(U)\log(V)
,
and by reasoning in a similar way we are led to think that
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
implies the
presence of weight two polygammas, and a natural guess is
\Psi_{2}(s,t)
.
40
40

40

Recall that
-\Psi_{1}^{2}+\Psi_{2}
,
i.e.the double box has at most
\log^{2}U\log^{2}V
.

In order to map the basis element
\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}
and
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
to Mellin space, we will do the following.
First let us write
\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(\frac{z}{z-1},\frac{\bar{z}}{{\bar{z}}-1})
in Mellin space by considering the expression

\frac{1}{(z-\bar{z})}\Bigg[z(1-z)\frac{\partial}{\partial z}+{\bar{z}}(1-{\bar{z}})\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{z}}}\Bigg]\Bigg[(z-\bar{z})\oint\frac{U^{s}V^{t}}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[s+t+1]^{2}{\cal K}^{(3)}(s,t)\Bigg]\;.

(F.37)

This gives the result

\oint\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[s+t+1]^{2}\Bigg[(s-t)U^{s}V^{t}+(1+s+t)(U^{s}V^{t+1}-U^{s+1}V^{t})\Bigg]{\cal K}^{(3)}(s,t)

(F.38)

which leads to

\oint\frac{U^{s}V^{t}}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[s+t+1]^{2}\Bigg[(s-t)+\frac{t^{2}{\cal K}^{(3)}(s,t-1)-s^{2}{\cal K}^{(3)}(s-1,t)}{s+t}\Bigg]

(F.39)

The Mellin amplitude simplifies to weight
3
polygammas, giving the result,

\frac{1}{3}\Bigg[\Psi^{3}_{1}(u,t)-\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{3}(u,t)+\pi^{2}\Psi_{1}\Bigg]\,.

(F.40)

This is the amplitude we used to define
K_{3}(s,t)
in (
4.60
). Finally, to get
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
we consider another
equation on
{\cal P}_{3}
such that, once decomposed on the basis, has a component along
{\cal P}_{1}\log^{2}(U)
.
For example

\frac{1}{(z-\bar{z})}\Bigg[z\frac{\partial}{\partial z}+\bar{z}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{z}}}\Bigg]\Bigg[(z-\bar{z})\oint\frac{U^{s}V^{t}}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\Gamma[-s]^{2}\Gamma[-t]^{2}\Gamma[s+t+1]^{2}{\cal K}^{(3)}(s,t)\Bigg]

(F.41)

(Note this is not
\partial_{+}{\cal P}_{3}(z,\bar{z})
.) With manipulation similar to the one above we find that the new
element that is needed to expand this function in a basis, is proportional to

\Psi_{2}-\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}

(F.42)

As expected we have found a weight 2 combination whose leading term is just
\Psi_{2}
. This is the amplitude we used to define
{\tilde{K}}_{2}(s,t)
in (
4.60
).
